# Universal strong Kadison–Kastler stability

Coordinate `EN-116-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M3-B-TO-H-0597` | `PM-289.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Universal-strong-Kadison-Kastler-stability-September-23-2026` | date 2026-09-23 | derived from the openai/math preprint Universal-strong-Kadison-Kastler-stability-September-23-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that sufficiently close unital von Neumann algebras are conjugate by a unitary arbitrarily close to the identity. The tolerance depends only on the prescribed distance of that unitary from the identity, uniformly over all algebras, representations, and Hilbert spaces. This resolves the strong Kadison–Kastler conjecture.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that sufficiently close unital von Neumann algebras are conjugate by a unitary arbitrarily close to the identity. The tolerance depends only on the prescribed distance of that unitary from the identity, uniformly over all algebras, representations, and Hilbert spaces. This resolves the strong Kadison–Kastler conjecture.

- **claim-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $\delta=\delta(\varepsilon)>0$ such that the following holds. If $H$ is any complex Hilbert space and $M,N\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ are unital von Neumann algebras with identity $I_H$ and $d(M,N)<\delta$, there is a unitary $u\in\mathcal B(H)$ satisfying $$uMu^*=N,\qquad \lVert u-I_H\rVert<\varepsilon.$$
  - Explanation: States the resolution of the strong Kadison–Kastler conjecture: close unital von Neumann algebras on one Hilbert space are conjugate by a unitary near the identity, with a tolerance depending only on the requested displacement.
- **claim-amenable-near-inclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-amenable)): Let $R,Q\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be von Neumann algebras with identity $I$, with $R$ amenable. If $R\subset_\gamma Q$ and $\gamma<1/100$, there is a unitary $v\in(R\cup Q)''$ such that $$vRv^*\subseteq Q,\qquad \lVert v-I\rVert\le150\gamma.$$ If also $Q\subset_\gamma R$ and $\gamma<1/101$, the same conclusion holds with $vRv^*=Q$.
  - Explanation: Christensen-type result: an amenable algebra nearly contained in another can be moved into it by a small unitary lying in the generated algebra; two-sided near inclusion gives equality.
- **claim-commutant-cb** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-commutant-cb)): For every unital von Neumann algebra $A\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ and $h\in\mathcal B(H)$, $$\label{eq:commutant-cb} \operatorname{dist}(h,A')\le\lVert\operatorname{ad}_h\|_A\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}.$$ Consequently $d_{\mathrm{cb}}(A,B)\le\gamma$ implies $d(A',B')\le4\gamma$.
  - Explanation: The distance from an operator to the commutant is controlled by the completely bounded norm of its inner derivation; consequently cb-close algebras have close commutants.
- **claim-close-representations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-paired)): Let $\rho,\theta:P\to\mathcal B(H)$ be normal unital $*$-representations of a von Neumann algebra. If $\lVert\rho-\theta\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le e<1/4$, there is a unitary $u\in\mathcal B(H)$ with $$u\rho(x)u^*=\theta(x)\quad(x\in P),\qquad \lVert u-I\rVert\le4e.$$
  - Explanation: Two normal unital representations that are cb-close are implemented by a unitary near the identity.
- **claim-ucp-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-ucp)): Let $P,Q$ be von Neumann algebras, let $\rho:P\to\mathcal B(H)$ be a faithful normal unital representation, and let $Q\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ have the same identity. Suppose that $F:P\to Q$ is UCP and $$\lVert F-\rho\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le e<10^{-3},\qquad \sup_{y\in Q_1}\operatorname{dist}(y,\rho(P)_1)\le g,\qquad g+e<1.$$ Then there is a normal unital $*$-isomorphism $\theta:P\to Q$ and a unitary $u\in\mathcal B(H)$ such that $$\lVert\theta-\rho\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le32e,\qquad u\rho(x)u^*=\theta(x)\ (x\in P),\qquad \lVert u-I\rVert\le128e.$$ The map $F$ need not be normal.
  - Explanation: A unital completely positive map close in cb norm to a faithful representation, with small reverse ordinary gap, is turned into a normal isomorphism and a spatial unitary; normality of the map is not needed.
- **claim-tensor-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tensor-gap)): Let $A,B\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be unital von Neumann algebras with $d_{\mathrm{cb}}(A,B)\le\gamma$. If $K$ is separable, then $$d_{\mathrm{cb}}(A\mathbin{\overline\otimes}\mathcal B(K),B\mathbin{\overline\otimes}\mathcal B(K))\le\gamma.$$ If $D\subseteq\mathcal B(K)$ is a von Neumann algebra whose commutant is the strong closure of increasing finite-dimensional unital algebras, then $$d_{\mathrm{cb}}(A\mathbin{\overline\otimes}D,B\mathbin{\overline\otimes}D)\le\gamma.$$
  - Explanation: The cb gap is not increased by tensoring with all bounded operators on a separable space or with an algebra whose commutant is an increasing limit of finite-dimensional algebras.
- **claim-invariant-mean** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-invariant-mean)): Let $G$ be a discrete amenable group and $m$ a left invariant mean on $\ell^\infty(G)$: thus $m$ is a positive unital linear functional with $m(f(h^{-1}\,\cdot))=m(f)$ for every $h\in G$. For a bounded family $T_g\in\mathcal B(K)$, define $$\langle (m_gT_g)\xi,\eta\rangle=m_g\langle T_g\xi,\eta\rangle.$$ This averaging map from bounded operator families to $\mathcal B(K)$ is UCP, commutes with multiplication on either side by a fixed operator, and preserves weak-operator closed linear subspaces. If $\lVert T_g-T\rVert\le a$ for every $g$, then $\lVert m_gT_g-T\rVert\le a$, also at rectangular matrix levels. No measurability of the family is required.* *For a unitary representation $g\mapsto V_g$ on $K$, the map $$E_V(T)=m_gV_gTV_g^*\qquad(T\in\mathcal B(K))$$ is a UCP projection onto $\{V_g:g\in G\}'$. It preserves every von Neumann algebra invariant under these conjugations. More generally, for an action by automorphisms $\alpha_g$ on a von Neumann algebra $P$, the formula $$\varphi(E(x))=m_g\varphi(\alpha_g(x))\qquad(\varphi\in P_*)$$ defines a UCP map onto $P^G$ fixing that algebra. These averaging maps need not be normal.
  - Explanation: Invariant means yield unital completely positive averaging maps on operator families, projections onto fixed-point algebras, with no measurability or normality required.
- **claim-uniform-amplification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-amplification)): There is an absolute constant $C_{\mathrm{amp}}$ such that, for every type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $M\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ on a separable Hilbert space and every selfadjoint $h\in\mathcal B(H)$, $$\lVert\operatorname{ad}_h\|_M\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le C_{\mathrm{amp}}\lVert\operatorname{ad}_h\|_M\rVert.$$ The constant is independent of $M$, its representation, and $H$.
  - Explanation: On a separably acting type II_1 factor the matrix amplifications of an inner derivation are bounded by an absolute multiple of its ordinary norm.
- **claim-column-estimate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-column-commutator)): Let $A\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra, let $h\in\mathcal B(H)$, and put $\delta=\lVert\operatorname{ad}_h\|_A\rVert$. Then $$\lVert([h,a_i])_i\rVert_{\mathrm{col}} \le 8\delta\,\lVert(a_i)_i\rVert_{\mathrm{col}} \qquad(a_1,\ldots,a_l\in A).$$
  - Explanation: Column norms of commutators are bounded by a fixed multiple of the derivation norm, via Haagerup's cyclic similarity estimate.
- **claim-trace-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-transfer)): Let $P\subseteq\mathcal B(K)$ be a finite von Neumann algebra with faithful normal trace $\tau$.* 1. *If $(x_j)$ is bounded and $\lim_{j\to\omega}\lVert x_j\rVert_2=0$, then $\lim_{j\to\omega}\lVert x_j\xi\rVert=0$ for every $\xi\in K$.* 2. *If $x=(x_j)_\omega\in P^\omega$, then the weak-operator ultralimit of $x_j$ in this representation is $E_P(x)$, where $E_P:P^\omega\to P$ is the trace-preserving conditional expectation.
  - Explanation: Trace-norm null sequences act as vector-null sequences in a normal representation, and weak limits of ultrapower elements are given by the conditional expectation.
- **claim-free-words** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-free-words)): For every integer $r\geq 1$ there are constants $C_r>0$ and an integer $d_r\geq r$ with the following property. Let $d\geq d_r$, and let $(\mathcal A,\tau)$ be a von Neumann algebra with a faithful normal tracial state. Suppose that $A_1,\ldots,A_r\subset\mathcal A$ are unital copies of $M_d(\mathbb C)$ which are scalar free: the trace of a product of centered letters from successively different copies is zero. For each $j$, let $(e_a^{(j)})_{a=1}^d$ be the diagonal minimal projections of $A_j$.* *For a multiset $W$ of cardinality $r$ drawn from $\{1,\ldots,d\}$, put $$B_W=\sum_{\substack{(a_1,\ldots,a_r)\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^r\\ \text{with multiset }W}} e_{a_r}^{(r)}\cdots e_{a_1}^{(1)}.$$ Then $\lVert B_W\rVert\leq\beta_W$, where $$\label{eq:fw-beta} \beta_W= \begin{cases} 7\sqrt{(r-1)!}\,d^{-(r-1)/2}, &\text{if all labels in $W$ are distinct},\\ C_r d^{-(r-1)/2},&\text{otherwise}. \end{cases}$$ The constants and threshold depend only on $r$, not on the ambient algebra, the free copies, or $W$.
  - Explanation: Norm bounds for sums of projection words with prescribed label multiplicities in scalar free matrix copies, with constants depending only on the word length.
- **claim-through-label** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-through-label)): For every integer $N\geq1$ there is an integer $d_0(N)$ such that, whenever $d\geq d_0(N)$, the constants <a href="#eq:fw-beta" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fw-beta">[eq:fw-beta]</a> satisfy $$\label{eq:fw-through-label} \max_{1\leq a\leq d} \sum_{\substack{W\text{ a multiset from }\{1,\ldots,d\}\\ \|W\|=r,\ a\in W}} \beta_W^2\leq64 \qquad(1\leq r\leq N).$$ Membership $a\in W$ here means that $a$ occurs at least once; each multiset is counted once, regardless of that multiplicity. In particular, for free copies as in <a href="#lem:free-words" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:free-words">11</a>, the norm bounds and <a href="#eq:fw-through-label" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fw-through-label">[eq:fw-through-label]</a> hold simultaneously through length $N$ after choosing this one threshold for $d$.
  - Explanation: For large matrix dimension, the sum of squared word bounds over all multisets containing a fixed label is uniformly bounded, simultaneously for all lengths up to the given cutoff.
- **claim-free-lifts** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-free-lifts)): Let $M\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be a separably acting $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor, let $n\geq1$, and put $P=M_n(M)$. For every free ultrafilter $\omega$ there is a Haar unitary $s\in P^\omega$ such that $W^*(s)$ and the constant copy of $P$ are scalar free. Moreover, $s$ has unitary representatives $$s=(s_j)_\omega,\qquad s_j\in\mathcal U(M_n(R_j)),$$ where each $R_j\subseteq M$ is a unital full matrix algebra. Consequently, for every $h=h^*\in\mathcal B(H)$, with $\delta=\lVert\operatorname{ad}_h\|_M\rVert$ and $h_n=I_n\otimes h$, these representatives satisfy $$\label{eq:free-lift-commutator} \lVert[h_n,s_j]\rVert\leq2\delta\qquad(j\geq1).$$
  - Explanation: Constructs in a tracial ultrapower a Haar unitary scalar free from the constant matrix algebra, with representatives in matrix subalgebras and controlled commutator with a selfadjoint operator.
- **claim-haar-twist** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-haar-twist)): Let $(F,\tau)$ be a finite tracial von Neumann algebra, let $P\subseteq F$ be a unital von Neumann subalgebra, and let $s\in F$ be Haar and scalar free from $P$. For every $U\in\mathcal U(P)$, both $sU$ and $Us$ are Haar and scalar free from $P$.
  - Explanation: Multiplying a free Haar unitary on either side by a unitary of the subalgebra keeps it Haar and scalar free from that subalgebra.
- **claim-boundary-mean** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-mean)): For every countable group $G$ there are a standard probability space $(D,\nu)$ with a nonsingular $G$-action and a positive unital contraction $$m:L^\infty(G\times D)\longrightarrow L^\infty(D)$$ satisfying $$\label{eq:boundary-mean-identities} m(\lambda_g F)=\alpha_g(mF),\qquad m(fF)=f\,m(F) \quad(f\in L^\infty(D)).$$ There is a mean $m^{(2)}$ with the same properties for the separate $G\times G$-action on $D\times D$ and functions on $G^2\times D^2$. Each mean extends to a contraction on bounded operator fields on any separable $K$. The extension preserves adjoints and von Neumann algebra values, and is a bimodule map over bounded operator fields independent of the group variable: $$\label{eq:boundary-operator-module} m(BFC)=B\,m(F)\,C.$$
  - Explanation: For any countable group, constructs a probability space with a nonsingular action and an equivariant module-compatible mean, extending to operator fields and to the product action.
- **claim-boundary-correction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-correction)): Let $D$ and $m$ be as in [lem:boundary-mean](#lem:boundary-mean), and let $A\subset\mathcal B(K)$ be a von Neumann algebra on a separable Hilbert space. Suppose $V_g\in L^\infty(D,\mathcal U(A))$, $g\in G$, and $$e=\sup_{g,h\in G}\lVert V_g\alpha_g(V_h)-V_{gh}\rVert_\infty \le\frac1{64}.$$ Then there are measurable unitary fields $W_g$ with $$\label{eq:boundary-exact-cocycle} W_g\alpha_g(W_h)=W_{gh},\qquad \sup_g\lVert W_g-V_g\rVert_\infty\le 2e.$$ In particular $W_1=I$. The identities hold simultaneously almost everywhere for every $g,h$, and may be used on a $G$-invariant conull subset of $D$.
  - Explanation: An approximate measurable unitary cocycle with small defect is corrected, by a quadratically convergent averaging iteration, to an exact cocycle close to it.
- **claim-boundary-intertwiner** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-intertwiner)): Take either $(\Gamma,Z)=(G,D)$ or $(\Gamma,Z)=(G^2,D^2)$, with its mean from [lem:boundary-mean](#lem:boundary-mean). Let $U:\Gamma\to\mathcal U(K)$ be a unitary representation on a separable Hilbert space, and let $\Sigma_s\in L^\infty(Z,\mathcal U(K))$ be an exact cocycle for the given action. If $$\sup_{s\in\Gamma}\lVert\Sigma_s-U_s\rVert_\infty\le\delta<1,$$ there is $L\in L^\infty(Z,\mathcal U(K))$ such that $$\label{eq:boundary-intertwiner} \lVert L-I\rVert_\infty\le2\delta,\qquad L(z)U_s=\Sigma_s(z)L(s^{-1}z).$$
  - Explanation: An exact boundary cocycle close to a unitary representation is intertwined with it by a unitary field close to the identity.
- **claim-boundary-diagonal-unitary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-diagonal-unitary)): Let $A$ be a finite von Neumann algebra with faithful normal tracial state $\tau$ and separable trace Hilbert space $L^2(A,\tau)$. Let $W_g\in L^\infty(D,\mathcal U(A))$ be an exact cocycle. Suppose $$\sup_{g\in G}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup}_{x,y\in D} \lVert W_g(x)W_g(y)^*-I\rVert\le r<1.$$ Then there is a measurable field $a:D^2\to\mathcal U(A)$ such that $$\label{eq:boundary-diagonal-unitary} \lVert a-I\rVert_\infty\le2r,\qquad a(x,y)=W_g(x)a(g^{-1}x,g^{-1}y)W_g(y)^*.$$ In particular, if $\sup_g\lVert W_g-u_g\rVert_\infty\le\delta$ for any constant unitaries $u_g$ on a common representation space, the conclusion holds with $\lVert a-I\rVert_\infty\le4\delta$ when $2\delta<1$.
  - Explanation: In a finite algebra, a least-norm element of a tracial convex hull produces a diagonally equivariant unitary field comparing the two boundary variables.
- **claim-double-ergodicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-double-ergodicity)): For the space $D$ constructed in [lem:boundary-mean](#lem:boundary-mean), let $\sigma:G\to\mathcal U(K)$ be a unitary representation on a separable Hilbert space. If a bounded measurable map $f_0:D^2\to K$ satisfies $$f_0(gx,gy)=\sigma(g)f_0(x,y)\quad(g\in G)$$ almost everywhere, then $f_0$ is almost everywhere a constant vector fixed by every $\sigma(g)$.
  - Explanation: Bounded measurable vector fields on the square of the boundary that transform under a unitary representation along the diagonal action are constant and invariant.
- **claim-finite-factors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-finite)): There are a number $t_{\mathrm{fin}}>0$ and a nondecreasing function $\eta_{\mathrm{fin}}:(0,t_{\mathrm{fin}})\to(0,\infty)$, with $\eta_{\mathrm{fin}}(t)\to0$ as $t\downarrow0$, having the following property. Let $H$ be a separable nonzero Hilbert space, let $M\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be a unital finite factor, and let $N\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be a von Neumann algebra with the same identity. If $d(M,N)<t<t_{\mathrm{fin}}$, then $N$ is a finite factor and there is a unitary $u\in\mathcal B(H)$ such that $$uMu^*=N,\qquad \lVert u-I_H\rVert\le\eta_{\mathrm{fin}}(t).$$ The threshold and function are independent of $M,N$ and their representations.
  - Explanation: Uniform stability for finite factors on separable spaces: a nearby algebra is a finite factor and is conjugate by a unitary near the identity, with threshold and modulus independent of the algebras.
- **claim-finite-neighbor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-neighbor)): If $M\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ is a unital finite factor and $N\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ is a von Neumann algebra with $d(M,N)<1/10$, then $N$ is a finite factor.
  - Explanation: Being a finite factor is stable under small Kadison–Kastler perturbation.
- **claim-irreducible-hyperfinite** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-irreducible-hyperfinite)): Every separable-predual $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $M$ contains an irreducible unital hyperfinite $\mathrm{II}_1$ subfactor $R$. One can write $R=(\bigcup_j Q_j)''$, where $Q_j$ are increasing unital full matrix algebras and their sizes tend to infinity.
  - Explanation: Popa-type result: every separable-predual II_1 factor has an irreducible hyperfinite subfactor presented by increasing matrix algebras.
- **claim-common-position** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-common-position)): Let $M,N$ be separable-predual $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors acting nondegenerately on one Hilbert space, with mutual strict $g$-near inclusions, where $0<g<1.74\cdot10^{-13}$. Suppose that an amenable von Neumann subalgebra $R\subseteq M\cap N$ satisfies $R'\cap M\subseteq R$. There are faithful normal representations $\pi:M\to\mathcal B(K)$ and $\rho:N\to\mathcal B(K)$, a common unit vector $\Omega$, and a number $\beta<50948\sqrt g$ such that:* 1. *both images are in standard tracial position with vector $\Omega$;* 2. *$\pi\|_R=\rho\|_R$, and the images have mutual strict $\beta$-near inclusions;* 3. *for original contractions $x\in M$, $y\in N$, $$\lVert\pi(x)-\rho(y)\rVert\le\beta+\lVert x-y\rVert;$$* 4. *if $e_R$ is the projection onto $\overline{\pi(R)\Omega}$, then $(\pi(M)\cup\{e_R\})''=(\rho(N)\cup\{e_R\})''$.* *The space $K$ can be taken separable.
  - Explanation: Close II_1 factors sharing an amenable subalgebra can be placed in a common standard tracial representation with square-root control of the new near inclusions.
- **claim-finite-right-action** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-right-action)): In the setting of [prop:finite-common-position](#prop:finite-common-position), the right actions of $R$ agree elementwise: $$J_M rJ_M=J_N rJ_N\qquad(r\in R).$$ In particular $T=J_NJ_M-I$ commutes with the left and right actions of $R$.
  - Explanation: In common position the right actions of the shared subfactor agree, so the difference of the two conjugations commutes with its actions.
- **claim-finite-ball-tests** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-ball-tests)): Let $M$ be a $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor and let $R=(\bigcup_jQ_j)''\subseteq M$ be as in [lem:irreducible-hyperfinite](#lem:irreducible-hyperfinite). If a bounded operator $V$ on $L^2(M,\tau)$ commutes with left and right multiplication by $R$, then $$\lVert V\rVert\le384\sup_{a,b\in M_1} \lvert\langle Va\Omega,b\Omega\rangle\rvert.$$
  - Explanation: A bimodular operator on the trace Hilbert space is bounded by a fixed multiple of its values on trace vectors of unit-ball elements, via random averaging.
- **claim-tracial-conjugations** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tracial-conjugations)): Let $M,N\subseteq\mathcal B(K)$ be $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors in common standard tracial position with vector $\Omega$. Suppose that $R\subseteq M\cap N$ is an irreducible hyperfinite subfactor of $M$, presented by increasing matrix algebras, and that its right actions agree. If $d(M,N)<b$, then $$\lVert J_M-J_N\rVert\le C_Jb,\qquad C_J=768.$$
  - Explanation: The two modular conjugations in common tracial position differ in norm by at most a fixed multiple of the Kadison–Kastler distance.
- **claim-jordan-orientation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-jordan-orientation)): A surjective linear Jordan homomorphism between unital complex algebras, whose target is a factor von Neumann algebra, is either a homomorphism or an antihomomorphism.
  - Explanation: Herstein-type dichotomy: a surjective Jordan homomorphism onto a factor is multiplicative or antimultiplicative.
- **claim-finite-standard-isomorphism** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-standard-isomorphism)): There are absolute constants $b_0>0$ and $C_{\mathrm{st}}>0$ such that the following holds. Let $M,N\subseteq\mathcal B(K)$ be type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors on a separable Hilbert space in common standard tracial position with vector $\Omega$. Suppose they contain a common hyperfinite $\mathrm{II}_1$ subfactor $R$, irreducible in $M$ and generated by increasing matrix algebras, and that $J_MrJ_M=J_NrJ_N$ for every $r\in R$. If $d(M,N)<b<b_0$, then there is a normal unital star-isomorphism $\vartheta:M\to N$ satisfying $$\sup_{x\in M_1}\lVert\vartheta(x)-x\rVert\le C_{\mathrm{st}}b.$$
  - Explanation: In common tracial position, close II_1 factors are joined by a normal isomorphism close to the identity in ordinary norm, built from boundary cocycles and a Jordan map.
- **claim-common-infinite-amplification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-common-infinite-amplification)): Let $M,N\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ contain the same unital copy of $\mathcal B(\ell^2)$. Then $d_{\mathrm{cb}}(M,N)=d(M,N)$.
  - Explanation: A common unital copy of the bounded operators on the square-summable sequences makes the cb distance equal to the ordinary distance.
- **claim-fixed-tensor-factor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fixed-tensor-factor)): One has $D_\rho'\cap D=\mathbb C1$. If $A$ is any factor with separable predual, then $A\mathbin{\overline\otimes}D$ is a type $\mathrm{III}_1$ factor and $$A\mathbin{\overline\otimes}D\cong(A\mathbin{\overline\otimes}D)\mathbin{\overline\otimes}R_\lambda,$$ where $R_\lambda$ is the Powers factor obtained using only $(M_2(\mathbb C),\rho_\lambda)$ sites.
  - Explanation: The fixed infinite tensor product has trivial relative commutant in its centralizer and turns any separable-predual factor into a type III_1 factor absorbing a Powers factor.
- **claim-prepared-pair-existence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-prepared-pair)): There are absolute constants $c,C>0$ with the following property. Suppose $M_0,N_0\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ are factors on a separable Hilbert space, $M_0$ is infinite, and $d(M_0,N_0)<t<c$. For the fixed $D\subseteq\mathcal B(K)$ above, there are unitaries $u_0\in\mathcal B(H)$ and $v\in\mathcal B(H\otimes K)$ satisfying $$\lVert u_0-1\rVert+\lVert v-1\rVert\leq Ct$$ such that $$M=M_0\mathbin{\overline\otimes}D,\qquad N=v\bigl((u_0N_0u_0^*)\mathbin{\overline\otimes}D\bigr)v^*$$ form a prepared pair and $d_{\mathrm{cb}}(M,N)\leq Ct$.
  - Explanation: A close pair of factors with one infinite is adjusted by small unitaries and tensored with the fixed factor to give a prepared pair with small cb gap.
- **claim-modular-graphs** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-modular-graphs)): There is an absolute constant $C$ such that every prepared pair with $d_{\mathrm{cb}}(M,N)\leq\gamma$ satisfies $$\sup_{c>0}\lVert E_c^M-E_c^N\rVert\leq C\gamma.$$
  - Explanation: Projections onto the rescaled Tomita graphs of a prepared pair are uniformly close over all positive rescalings.
- **claim-modular-cuts** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-modular-cuts)): For a prepared pair with $d_{\mathrm{cb}}(M,N)\leq\gamma$, put $L_P=\log\Delta_P$, $P=M,N$. There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for every $a\in\mathbb R$, $$\lVert 1_{(-\infty,a-1/2]}(L_N) 1_{[a+1/2,\infty)}(L_M)\rVert\leq C\gamma.$$ The same estimate holds with $M,N$ interchanged.
  - Explanation: Spectral cuts of the logarithmic modular operators separated by a fixed gap nearly annihilate each other.
- **claim-modular-alignment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-modular-alignment)): There are absolute constants $c_{\rm mod},C_{\rm mod}>0$ with the following property. Let $(M,N,\Omega,R)$ be a prepared pair as in [def:prepared-pair](#def:prepared-pair), acting on the same separable Hilbert space $H$, and suppose that $d_{\mathrm{cb}}(M,N)<\gamma<c_{\rm mod}$. There is a unitary $q\in\mathcal B(H)$ such that $$\lVert q-I\rVert\le C_{\rm mod}\gamma, \qquad d_{\mathrm{cb}}(qMq^*,N)\le C_{\rm mod}\gamma, \qquad \Delta_N^{it}(qMq^*)\Delta_N^{-it}=qMq^* \quad(t\in\mathbb R).$$ The algebra $N$ and its modular group are unchanged.
  - Explanation: A small change of position makes the first algebra invariant under the modular group of the second, which stays unchanged.
- **claim-infinite-commutant-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-infinite-commutant-bound)): For a separably acting properly infinite von Neumann algebra $P\subset\mathcal B(H)$ and $h=h^*\in\mathcal B(H)$, $$\operatorname{dist}(h,P')\le4\lVert\operatorname{ad}_h\|_P\rVert.$$
  - Explanation: For properly infinite algebras the distance of a selfadjoint operator to the commutant is bounded by a fixed multiple of the ordinary derivation norm.
- **claim-normalizer-linear** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-normalizer-linear)): There is an absolute $K<\infty$ such that every separably acting type $\mathrm{III}$ factor $P\subset\mathcal B(H)$ and every self-adjoint $h\in\mathcal B(H)$ satisfy $$\operatorname{dist}(h,P+P') \le K\sup_{x\in P_1}\operatorname{dist}([h,x],P). \label{eq:normalizer-linear-bound}$$ The sum $P+P'$ here is a linear subspace; no closedness assertion about that sum is needed.
  - Explanation: Uniform infinitesimal estimate: distance to the sum of a type III factor and its commutant is controlled by one-sided commutator defects.
- **claim-normalizer-path** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-normalizer-path)): There are absolute constants $e_0,C_{\rm path}>0$ such that the following holds. Let $P\subset\mathcal B(H)$ be a separably acting type $\mathrm{III}$ factor, let $I\subset\mathbb R$ be an interval containing $0$, and let $v:I\to\mathcal U(\mathcal B(H))$ be norm continuous with $v(0)=I$. If $0\le e<e_0$ and $$\sup_{t\in I}\sup_{x\in P_1}\operatorname{dist}(v(t)xv(t)^*,P)\le e,$$ then $$\sup_{t\in I}\operatorname{dist}(v(t),\mathcal G_P)\le C_{\rm path}e.$$ The constants are independent of the interval’s length.
  - Explanation: A norm-continuous path of approximate normalizers from the identity stays uniformly close to the product group of the factor and its commutant.
- **claim-analytic-multiplier** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-analytic-multiplier)): Suppose multiplication by a bounded field $B(t)$ on $L^2(\mathbb R,H)$ preserves $P_0\mathcal K$. Then it is the boundary field of a bounded, operator-norm holomorphic function on the upper half-plane, given by $$\label{eq:analytic-poisson} B(t+ir)=\int_\mathbb R\frac{r}{\pi((t-s)^2+r^2)}B(s)\,ds, \qquad r>0.$$ Its norm is at most $\mathop{\rm ess\,sup}_s\lVert B(s)\rVert$. For two such fields, and any constant $x\in\mathcal B(H)$, the extension of the boundary product $B(s)xS(s)$ is $B(z)xS(z)$.
  - Explanation: A bounded multiplier preserving the positive-frequency subspace extends to a bounded holomorphic field on the upper half-plane through the Poisson kernel, compatibly with products.
- **claim-modular-near-normalizers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-modular-near-normalizers)): Let $M,N\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be a prepared pair on a separable Hilbert space, with $M$ of type $\mathrm{III}$ and $d_{\mathrm{cb}}(M,N)\leq\gamma$. Write $$U_P(t)=e^{itL_P},\qquad L_P=\log\Delta_P\quad(P=M,N), \qquad W(t)=U_N(t)U_M(t)^*.$$ Assume the separated-cut estimate of [cor:modular-cuts](#cor:modular-cuts), with its universal constant, and the local path estimate of [lem:local-normalizer-path](#lem:local-normalizer-path). There are universal constants $\gamma_0>0$ and $C$ such that, if $0<\gamma<\gamma_0$, then for every $t\in\mathbb R$ there is a unitary $n_t$ satisfying $$n_tMn_t^*=M,\qquad \lVert n_t-W(t)\rVert\leq C\gamma.$$ No regularity of the choices $t\mapsto n_t$ is asserted or needed.
  - Explanation: Modular cut estimates yield, at each time, an exact normalizer of the algebra close to the difference of the two modular unitaries.
- **claim-small-exact-normalizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-small-exact-normalizer)): There are absolute $r_0,C_0>0$ such that, if $P$ is a separably acting type $\mathrm{III}$ factor, $v\in\mathcal N_P$, and $\lVert v-I\rVert<r_0$, then $$v=ab',\qquad a\in\mathcal U(P),\quad b'\in\mathcal U(P'), \qquad \lVert a-I\rVert+\lVert b'-I\rVert\le C_0\lVert v-I\rVert.$$
  - Explanation: An exact normalizer near the identity factors as a commuting product of a unitary of the factor and one of its commutant.
- **claim-normalizer-representation-correction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-normalizer-representation-correction)): There are absolute $e_1,C_1>0$ such that the following holds. Let $P\subset\mathcal B(H)$ be a separably acting type $\mathrm{III}$ factor. For any family $v_t\in\mathcal N_P$, $t\in\mathbb R$, with $$e:=\sup_{t,s\in\mathbb R}\lVert v_tv_s-v_{t+s}\rVert<e_1,$$ there is an exact unitary representation $z:\mathbb R\to\mathcal N_P$ of the discrete group with $$\sup_t\lVert z_t-v_t\rVert\le C_1e.$$
  - Explanation: An approximate representation of the real line by normalizers is corrected, by iterated averaging, to an exact representation inside the normalizer group.
- **claim-core-fixed-points** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-core-fixed-points)): With this notation, $$\label{eq:core-fixed-description} C(P,U)= \bigl(P\mathbin{\overline\otimes}\mathcal B(L^2(\mathbb R))\bigr)^\alpha, \qquad \alpha_s=\operatorname{Ad}(U_s\otimes\rho_s).$$ For $(Q_a\xi)(t)=e^{iat}\xi(t)$, character conjugation $\beta_a=\operatorname{Ad}(Q_a)$ preserves $C(P,U)$, and $$\label{eq:core-character-fixed} C(P,U)^\beta=\pi(P).$$
  - Explanation: The concrete crossed product is a fixed-point algebra of a tensor action, and the base algebra is recovered as the fixed points of character conjugation.
- **claim-core-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-core-gap)): Let $P,Q\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be normalized by the same group $U$. If $d_{\mathrm{cb}}(P,Q)<\gamma$, then $$d_{\mathrm{cb}}(C(P,U),C(Q,U))\le\gamma.$$ The conclusion holds with any strict upper bound in place of $\gamma$.
  - Explanation: Common crossed products by a shared normalizing group inherit the cb gap of the base algebras.
- **claim-core-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-core-descent)): There are an absolute $\gamma_0>0$ and a nondecreasing function $\zeta:(0,\gamma_0)\to(0,\infty)$, with $\zeta(\gamma)\to0$ as $\gamma\downarrow0$, with the following property. Suppose $P,Q\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ are unital von Neumann algebras on a separable Hilbert space, $d_{\mathrm{cb}}(P,Q)<\gamma<\gamma_0$, and a strongly continuous unitary group $U$ normalizes both. If $C(Q,U)$ is a type $\mathrm{II}_\infty$ factor, then a unitary $a\in\mathcal B(H)$ satisfies $$aPa^*=Q,\qquad \lVert a-I\rVert\le\zeta(\gamma).$$ No factoriality or modular interpretation of $C(P,U)$ is assumed.
  - Explanation: Conjugacy of a finite corner inflates to the crossed products and descends, via an averaging UCP map and the UCP criterion, to the original algebras.
- **claim-separable-factors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-factors)): There are an absolute $t_{\rm fac}>0$ and a nondecreasing function $\eta_{\rm fac}:(0,t_{\rm fac})\to(0,\infty)$ with $\eta_{\rm fac}(t)\to0$ as $t\downarrow0$ such that the following holds. For every separable complex Hilbert space $H$ and every pair of unital factors $M_0,N_0\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ with the same identity and $d(M_0,N_0)<t<t_{\rm fac}$, there is a unitary $u\in\mathcal B(H)$ with $$uM_0u^*=N_0,\qquad \lVert u-I_H\rVert\le\eta_{\rm fac}(t).$$ The function and the threshold are independent of the factors, their types, and their representations.
  - Explanation: Uniform stability for all separably acting factors of any type, with threshold and modulus independent of the factors.
- **claim-centers-close** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-centers-close)): For unital von Neumann algebras $M,N\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$, $$d\bigl(Z(M),Z(N)\bigr)\leq 3d(M,N).$$ Consequently, whenever $d(M,N)<g<1/303$, there is a unitary $w\in\mathcal B(H)$ such that $$wZ(M)w^*=Z(N),\qquad \lVert w-I\rVert\leq450g.$$ For $N_0=w^*Nw$ one then has $Z(N_0)=Z(M)$ and $d(M,N_0)<901g$.
  - Explanation: Centers of close algebras are close, and a small unitary aligns them, so one may assume the same center.
- **claim-fiber-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fiber-gap)): Let $M,N\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ act on a separable Hilbert space and have the same center. In their common central decomposition, $$d(M_t,N_t)\leq d(M,N) \quad\text{for almost every }t.$$
  - Explanation: In a common central decomposition, almost every fiber pair is at most as far apart as the whole pair.
- **claim-measurable-implementers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-measurable-implementers)): In the setting of [lem:fiber-gap](#lem:fiber-gap), suppose that for some $a>0$ and almost every $t$ there is a unitary $v_t\in\mathcal B(H_t)$ satisfying $$v_t M_t v_t^*=N_t,\qquad\lVert v_t-I\rVert<a.$$ Then there is a unitary $v\in\mathcal B(H)$ such that $vMv^*=N$ and $\lVert v-I\rVert\leq a$.
  - Explanation: Fiberwise implementing unitaries near the identity can be selected measurably and assembled into one implementing unitary with the same bound.
- **claim-separable-algebras** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-separable-algebras)): For every $a>0$ there is $\delta_{\mathrm{sep}}(a)>0$ such that whenever $M,N$ are unital von Neumann algebras on a separable Hilbert space and $d(M,N)<\delta_{\mathrm{sep}}(a)$, there is a unitary $u$ with $$uMu^*=N,\qquad \lVert u-I\rVert<a.$$ The tolerance is independent of the algebras and the representation.
  - Explanation: Uniform stability for arbitrary unital von Neumann algebras on a separable Hilbert space, assembled from factors via aligned centers and measurable selection.
- **claim-separable-tests** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-separable-tests)): Let $M,N\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be unital von Neumann algebras, and let $g>d(M,N)$. Given finite sets $E\subseteq M$, $F\subseteq N$, and $V\subseteq H$ with $V$ containing a nonzero vector, there are separable unital $C^*$-subalgebras $A\subseteq M$, $B\subseteq N$ and a nonzero separable closed subspace $K\subseteq H$ such that:* 1. *$E\subseteq A$, $F\subseteq B$, and $V\subseteq K$;* 2. *$K$ reduces both $A$ and $B$;* 3. *$d(A,B)<g$ and $$d\bigl((A\|_K)'',(B\|_K)''\bigr)<g.$$* *Here the restriction homomorphisms are not required to be faithful.
  - Explanation: Finite tests in an arbitrary representation can be captured by separable subalgebras and a reducing separable subspace preserving the gap before and after restriction.
- **claim-uniform-matrix-commutant** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-uniform-matrix-commutant)): For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $\delta>0$, independent of the algebras, their representations, and their Hilbert spaces, such that unital von Neumann algebras $M,N\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ with the same identity and $d(M,N)<\delta$ satisfy $$d_{\mathrm{cb}}(M,N)<\varepsilon,\qquad d(M',N')<\varepsilon.$$
  - Explanation: Ordinary closeness uniformly forces cb closeness and closeness of commutants, with tolerance independent of algebras, representations and Hilbert spaces.

```json
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a discrete amenable group and $m$ a left invariant mean on $\\ell^\\infty(G)$: thus $m$ is a positive unital linear functional with $m(f(h^{-1}\\,\\cdot))=m(f)$ for every $h\\in G$. For a bounded family $T_g\\in\\mathcal B(K)$, define $$\\langle (m_gT_g)\\xi,\\eta\\rangle=m_g\\langle T_g\\xi,\\eta\\rangle.$$ This averaging map from bounded operator families to $\\mathcal B(K)$ is UCP, commutes with multiplication on either side by a fixed operator, and preserves weak-operator closed linear subspaces. If $\\lVert T_g-T\\rVert\\le a$ for every $g$, then $\\lVert m_gT_g-T\\rVert\\le a$, also at rectangular matrix levels. No measurability of the family is required.*\n\n*For a unitary representation $g\\mapsto V_g$ on $K$, the map $$E_V(T)=m_gV_gTV_g^*\\qquad(T\\in\\mathcal B(K))$$ is a UCP projection onto $\\{V_g:g\\in G\\}'$. It preserves every von Neumann algebra invariant under these conjugations. More generally, for an action by automorphisms $\\alpha_g$ on a von Neumann algebra $P$, the formula $$\\varphi(E(x))=m_g\\varphi(\\alpha_g(x))\\qquad(\\varphi\\in P_*)$$ defines a UCP map onto $P^G$ fixing that algebra. These averaging maps need not be normal.",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

For a bounded family $T_g\\in\\mathcal B(K)$, define $$\\langle (m_gT_g)\\xi,\\eta\\rangle=m_g\\langle T_g\\xi,\\eta\\rangle.$$ This averaging map from bounded operator families to $\\mathcal B(K)$ is UCP, commutes with multiplication on either side by a fixed operator, and preserves weak-operator closed linear subspaces. If $\\lVert T_g-T\\rVert\\le a$ for every $g$, then $\\lVert m_gT_g-T\\rVert\\le a$, also at rectangular matrix levels. No measurability of the family is required.*\n\n*For a unitary representation $g\\mapsto V_g$ on $K$, the map $$E_V(T)=m_gV_gTV_g^*\\qquad(T\\in\\mathcal B(K))$$ is a UCP projection onto $\\{V_g:g\\in G\\}'$. It preserves every von Neumann algebra invariant under these conjugations. More generally, for an action by automorphisms $\\alpha_g$ on a von Neumann algebra $P$, the formula $$\\varphi(E(x))=m_g\\varphi(\\alpha_g(x))\\qquad(\\varphi\\in P_*)$$ defines a UCP map onto $P^G$ fixing that algebra. These averaging maps need not be normal.",

These averaging maps need not be normal.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-amplification",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $C_{\\mathrm{amp}}$ such that, for every type $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $M\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ on a separable Hilbert space and every selfadjoint $h\\in\\mathcal B(H)$, $$\\lVert\\operatorname{ad}_h|_M\\rVert_{\\mathrm{cb}}\\le\n C_{\\mathrm{amp}}\\lVert\\operatorname{ad}_h|_M\\rVert.$$ The constant is independent of $M$, its representation, and $H$.",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-amplification",
          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $C_{\\mathrm{amp}}$ such that, for every type $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $M\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ on a separable Hilbert space and every selfadjoint $h\\in\\mathcal B(H)$, $$\\lVert\\operatorname{ad}_h|_M\\rVert_{\\mathrm{cb}}\\le\n C_{\\mathrm{amp}}\\lVert\\operatorname{ad}_h|_M\\rVert.$$ The constant is independent of $M$, its representation, and $H$.",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "There is an absolute constant $C_{\\mathrm{amp}}$ such that, for every type $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $M\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ on a separable Hilbert space and every selfadjoint $h\\in\\mathcal B(H)$, $$\\lVert\\operatorname{ad}_h|_M\\rVert_{\\mathrm{cb}}\\le\n C_{\\mathrm{amp}}\\lVert\\operatorname{ad}_h|_M\\rVert.$$ The constant is independent of $M$, its representation, and $H$.",
      "explanation": "On a separably acting type II_1 factor the matrix amplifications of an inner derivation are bounded by an absolute multiple of its ordinary norm.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-trace-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P\\subseteq\\mathcal B(K)$ be a finite von Neumann algebra with faithful normal trace $\\tau$.*\n\n1.  *If $(x_j)$ is bounded and $\\lim_{j\\to\\omega}\\lVert x_j\\rVert_2=0$, then $\\lim_{j\\to\\omega}\\lVert x_j\\xi\\rVert=0$ for every $\\xi\\in K$.*\n\n2.  *If $x=(x_j)_\\omega\\in P^\\omega$, then the weak-operator ultralimit of $x_j$ in this representation is $E_P(x)$, where $E_P:P^\\omega\\to P$ is the trace-preserving conditional expectation.",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-trace-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $P\\subseteq\\mathcal B(K)$ be a finite von Neumann algebra with faithful normal trace $\\tau$.*\n\n1.  *If $(x_j)$ is bounded and $\\lim_{j\\to\\omega}\\lVert x_j\\rVert_2=0$, then $\\lim_{j\\to\\omega}\\lVert x_j\\xi\\rVert=0$ for every $\\xi\\in K$.*\n\n2.  *If $x=(x_j)_\\omega\\in P^\\omega$, then the weak-operator ultralimit of $x_j$ in this representation is $E_P(x)$, where $E_P:P^\\omega\\to P$ is the trace-preserving conditional expectation.",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $P\\subseteq\\mathcal B(K)$ be a finite von Neumann algebra with faithful normal trace $\\tau$.*\n\n1.  *If $(x_j)$ is bounded and $\\lim_{j\\to\\omega}\\lVert x_j\\rVert_2=0$, then $\\lim_{j\\to\\omega}\\lVert x_j\\xi\\rVert=0$ for every $\\xi\\in K$.*\n\n2.  *If $x=(x_j)_\\omega\\in P^\\omega$, then the weak-operator ultralimit of $x_j$ in this representation is $E_P(x)$, where $E_P:P^\\omega\\to P$ is the trace-preserving conditional expectation.",
      "explanation": "Trace-norm null sequences act as vector-null sequences in a normal representation, and weak limits of ultrapower elements are given by the conditional expectation.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-free-words",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $r\\geq 1$ there are constants $C_r>0$ and an integer $d_r\\geq r$ with the following property. Let $d\\geq d_r$, and let $(\\mathcal A,\\tau)$ be a von Neumann algebra with a faithful normal tracial state. Suppose that $A_1,\\ldots,A_r\\subset\\mathcal A$ are unital copies of $M_d(\\mathbb C)$ which are scalar free: the trace of a product of centered letters from successively different copies is zero. For each $j$, let $(e_a^{(j)})_{a=1}^d$ be the diagonal minimal projections of $A_j$.*\n\n\n*For a multiset $W$ of cardinality $r$ drawn from $\\{1,\\ldots,d\\}$, put $$B_W=\\sum_{\\substack{(a_1,\\ldots,a_r)\\in\\{1,\\ldots,d\\}^r\\\\\n                     \\text{with multiset }W}}\n       e_{a_r}^{(r)}\\cdots e_{a_1}^{(1)}.$$ Then $\\lVert B_W\\rVert\\leq\\beta_W$, where $$\\label{eq:fw-beta}\n \\beta_W=\n \\begin{cases}\n  7\\sqrt{(r-1)!}\\,d^{-(r-1)/2},\n       &\\text{if all labels in $W$ are distinct},\\\\\n  C_r d^{-(r-1)/2},&\\text{otherwise}.\n \\end{cases}$$ The constants and threshold depend only on $r$, not on the ambient algebra, the free copies, or $W$.",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-free-words",
          "exact_quote": "For every integer $r\\geq 1$ there are constants $C_r>0$ and an integer $d_r\\geq r$ with the following property. Let $d\\geq d_r$, and let $(\\mathcal A,\\tau)$ be a von Neumann algebra with a faithful normal tracial state. Suppose that $A_1,\\ldots,A_r\\subset\\mathcal A$ are unital copies of $M_d(\\mathbb C)$ which are scalar free: the trace of a product of centered letters from successively different copies is zero. For each $j$, let $(e_a^{(j)})_{a=1}^d$ be the diagonal minimal projections of $A_j$.*\n\n\n*For a multiset $W$ of cardinality $r$ drawn from $\\{1,\\ldots,d\\}$, put $$B_W=\\sum_{\\substack{(a_1,\\ldots,a_r)\\in\\{1,\\ldots,d\\}^r\\\\\n                     \\text{with multiset }W}}\n       e_{a_r}^{(r)}\\cdots e_{a_1}^{(1)}.$$ Then $\\lVert B_W\\rVert\\leq\\beta_W$, where $$\\label{eq:fw-beta}\n \\beta_W=\n \\begin{cases}\n  7\\sqrt{(r-1)!}\\,d^{-(r-1)/2},\n       &\\text{if all labels in $W$ are distinct},\\\\\n  C_r d^{-(r-1)/2},&\\text{otherwise}.\n \\end{cases}$$ The constants and threshold depend only on $r$, not on the ambient algebra, the free copies, or $W$.",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every integer $r\\geq 1$ there are constants $C_r>0$ and an integer $d_r\\geq r$ with the following property. Let $d\\geq d_r$, and let $(\\mathcal A,\\tau)$ be a von Neumann algebra with a faithful normal tracial state. Suppose that $A_1,\\ldots,A_r\\subset\\mathcal A$ are unital copies of $M_d(\\mathbb C)$ which are scalar free: the trace of a product of centered letters from successively different copies is zero. For each $j$, let $(e_a^{(j)})_{a=1}^d$ be the diagonal minimal projections of $A_j$.*\n\n\n*For a multiset $W$ of cardinality $r$ drawn from $\\{1,\\ldots,d\\}$, put $$B_W=\\sum_{\\substack{(a_1,\\ldots,a_r)\\in\\{1,\\ldots,d\\}^r\\\\\n                     \\text{with multiset }W}}\n       e_{a_r}^{(r)}\\cdots e_{a_1}^{(1)}.$$ Then $\\lVert B_W\\rVert\\leq\\beta_W$, where $$\\label{eq:fw-beta}\n \\beta_W=\n \\begin{cases}\n  7\\sqrt{(r-1)!}\\,d^{-(r-1)/2},\n       &\\text{if all labels in $W$ are distinct},\\\\\n  C_r d^{-(r-1)/2},&\\text{otherwise}.\n \\end{cases}$$ The constants and threshold depend only on $r$, not on the ambient algebra, the free copies, or $W$.",
      "explanation": "Norm bounds for sums of projection words with prescribed label multiplicities in scalar free matrix copies, with constants depending only on the word length.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-free-words",
          "exact_quote": "Let $d\\geq d_r$",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-free-words",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $A_1,\\ldots,A_r\\subset\\mathcal A$ are unital copies of $M_d(\\mathbb C)$",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-free-words",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-free-words",
          "exact_quote": "For every integer $r\\geq 1$",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-free-words"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-through-label",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $N\\geq1$ there is an integer $d_0(N)$ such that, whenever $d\\geq d_0(N)$, the constants <a href=\"#eq:fw-beta\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fw-beta\">[eq:fw-beta]</a> satisfy $$\\label{eq:fw-through-label}\n \\max_{1\\leq a\\leq d}\n \\sum_{\\substack{W\\text{ a multiset from }\\{1,\\ldots,d\\}\\\\\n                 |W|=r,\\ a\\in W}}\n       \\beta_W^2\\leq64\n \\qquad(1\\leq r\\leq N).$$ Membership $a\\in W$ here means that $a$ occurs at least once; each multiset is counted once, regardless of that multiplicity. In particular, for free copies as in <a href=\"#lem:free-words\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:free-words\">11</a>, the norm bounds and <a href=\"#eq:fw-through-label\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fw-through-label\">[eq:fw-through-label]</a> hold simultaneously through length $N$ after choosing this one threshold for $d$.",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-through-label",
          "exact_quote": "For every integer $N\\geq1$ there is an integer $d_0(N)$ such that, whenever $d\\geq d_0(N)$, the constants <a href=\"#eq:fw-beta\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fw-beta\">[eq:fw-beta]</a> satisfy $$\\label{eq:fw-through-label}\n \\max_{1\\leq a\\leq d}\n \\sum_{\\substack{W\\text{ a multiset from }\\{1,\\ldots,d\\}\\\\\n                 |W|=r,\\ a\\in W}}\n       \\beta_W^2\\leq64\n \\qquad(1\\leq r\\leq N).$$ Membership $a\\in W$ here means that $a$ occurs at least once; each multiset is counted once, regardless of that multiplicity. In particular, for free copies as in <a href=\"#lem:free-words\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:free-words\">11</a>, the norm bounds and <a href=\"#eq:fw-through-label\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fw-through-label\">[eq:fw-through-label]</a> hold simultaneously through length $N$ after choosing this one threshold for $d$.",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every integer $N\\geq1$ there is an integer $d_0(N)$ such that, whenever $d\\geq d_0(N)$, the constants <a href=\"#eq:fw-beta\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fw-beta\">[eq:fw-beta]</a> satisfy $$\\label{eq:fw-through-label}\n \\max_{1\\leq a\\leq d}\n \\sum_{\\substack{W\\text{ a multiset from }\\{1,\\ldots,d\\}\\\\\n                 |W|=r,\\ a\\in W}}\n       \\beta_W^2\\leq64\n \\qquad(1\\leq r\\leq N).$$ Membership $a\\in W$ here means that $a$ occurs at least once; each multiset is counted once, regardless of that multiplicity. In particular, for free copies as in <a href=\"#lem:free-words\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:free-words\">11</a>, the norm bounds and <a href=\"#eq:fw-through-label\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fw-through-label\">[eq:fw-through-label]</a> hold simultaneously through length $N$ after choosing this one threshold for $d$.",
      "explanation": "For large matrix dimension, the sum of squared word bounds over all multisets containing a fixed label is uniformly bounded, simultaneously for all lengths up to the given cutoff.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-through-label",
          "exact_quote": "whenever $d\\geq d_0(N)$",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-through-label",
      "kind": "bound",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-through-label",
          "exact_quote": "For every integer $N\\geq1$",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-cor-through-label"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-free-lifts",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ be a separably acting $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factor, let $n\\geq1$, and put $P=M_n(M)$. For every free ultrafilter $\\omega$ there is a Haar unitary $s\\in P^\\omega$ such that $W^*(s)$ and the constant copy of $P$ are scalar free. Moreover, $s$ has unitary representatives $$s=(s_j)_\\omega,\\qquad\n s_j\\in\\mathcal U(M_n(R_j)),$$ where each $R_j\\subseteq M$ is a unital full matrix algebra. Consequently, for every $h=h^*\\in\\mathcal B(H)$, with $\\delta=\\lVert\\operatorname{ad}_h|_M\\rVert$ and $h_n=I_n\\otimes h$, these representatives satisfy $$\\label{eq:free-lift-commutator}\n \\lVert[h_n,s_j]\\rVert\\leq2\\delta\\qquad(j\\geq1).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-free-lifts",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ be a separably acting $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factor, let $n\\geq1$, and put $P=M_n(M)$. For every free ultrafilter $\\omega$ there is a Haar unitary $s\\in P^\\omega$ such that $W^*(s)$ and the constant copy of $P$ are scalar free. Moreover, $s$ has unitary representatives $$s=(s_j)_\\omega,\\qquad\n s_j\\in\\mathcal U(M_n(R_j)),$$ where each $R_j\\subseteq M$ is a unital full matrix algebra. Consequently, for every $h=h^*\\in\\mathcal B(H)$, with $\\delta=\\lVert\\operatorname{ad}_h|_M\\rVert$ and $h_n=I_n\\otimes h$, these representatives satisfy $$\\label{eq:free-lift-commutator}\n \\lVert[h_n,s_j]\\rVert\\leq2\\delta\\qquad(j\\geq1).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $M\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ be a separably acting $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factor, let $n\\geq1$, and put $P=M_n(M)$. Consequently, for every $h=h^*\\in\\mathcal B(H)$, with $\\delta=\\lVert\\operatorname{ad}_h|_M\\rVert$ and $h_n=I_n\\otimes h$, these representatives satisfy $$\\label{eq:free-lift-commutator}\n \\lVert[h_n,s_j]\\rVert\\leq2\\delta\\qquad(j\\geq1).$$",
      "explanation": "Constructs in a tracial ultrapower a Haar unitary scalar free from the constant matrix algebra, with representatives in matrix subalgebras and controlled commutator with a selfadjoint operator.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-haar-twist",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(F,\\tau)$ be a finite tracial von Neumann algebra, let $P\\subseteq F$ be a unital von Neumann subalgebra, and let $s\\in F$ be Haar and scalar free from $P$. For every $U\\in\\mathcal U(P)$, both $sU$ and $Us$ are Haar and scalar free from $P$.",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-haar-twist",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(F,\\tau)$ be a finite tracial von Neumann algebra, let $P\\subseteq F$ be a unital von Neumann subalgebra, and let $s\\in F$ be Haar and scalar free from $P$. For every $U\\in\\mathcal U(P)$, both $sU$ and $Us$ are Haar and scalar free from $P$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-mean",
        "exact_quote": "For every countable group $G$ there are a standard probability space $(D,\\nu)$ with a nonsingular $G$-action and a positive unital contraction $$m:L^\\infty(G\\times D)\\longrightarrow L^\\infty(D)$$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:boundary-mean-identities}\n m(\\lambda_g F)=\\alpha_g(mF),\\qquad m(fF)=f\\,m(F)\n \\quad(f\\in L^\\infty(D)).$$ There is a mean $m^{(2)}$ with the same properties for the separate $G\\times G$-action on $D\\times D$ and functions on $G^2\\times D^2$. Each mean extends to a contraction on bounded operator fields on any separable $K$. The extension preserves adjoints and von Neumann algebra values, and is a bimodule map over bounded operator fields independent of the group variable: $$\\label{eq:boundary-operator-module}\n m(BFC)=B\\,m(F)\\,C.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },

        {

Each mean extends to a contraction on bounded operator fields on any separable $K$. The extension preserves adjoints and von Neumann algebra values, and is a bimodule map over bounded operator fields independent of the group variable: $$\\label{eq:boundary-operator-module}\n m(BFC)=B\\,m(F)\\,C.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }

        "exact_quote": "Let $D$ and $m$ be as in [lem:boundary-mean](#lem:boundary-mean), and let $A\\subset\\mathcal B(K)$ be a von Neumann algebra on a separable Hilbert space.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-intertwiner",
        "exact_quote": "Take either $(\\Gamma,Z)=(G,D)$ or $(\\Gamma,Z)=(G^2,D^2)$, with its mean from [lem:boundary-mean](#lem:boundary-mean). Let $U:\\Gamma\\to\\mathcal U(K)$ be a unitary representation on a separable Hilbert space, and let $\\Sigma_s\\in L^\\infty(Z,\\mathcal U(K))$ be an exact cocycle for the given action. If $$\\sup_{s\\in\\Gamma}\\lVert\\Sigma_s-U_s\\rVert_\\infty\\le\\delta<1,$$ there is $L\\in L^\\infty(Z,\\mathcal U(K))$ such that $$\\label{eq:boundary-intertwiner}\n \\lVert L-I\\rVert_\\infty\\le2\\delta,\\qquad\n L(z)U_s=\\Sigma_s(z)L(s^{-1}z).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-diagonal-unitary",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a finite von Neumann algebra with faithful normal tracial state $\\tau$ and separable trace Hilbert space $L^2(A,\\tau)$. Let $W_g\\in L^\\infty(D,\\mathcal U(A))$ be an exact cocycle. Suppose $$\\sup_{g\\in G}\\mathop{\\rm ess\\,sup}_{x,y\\in D}\n \\lVert W_g(x)W_g(y)^*-I\\rVert\\le r<1.$$ Then there is a measurable field $a:D^2\\to\\mathcal U(A)$ such that $$\\label{eq:boundary-diagonal-unitary}\n \\lVert a-I\\rVert_\\infty\\le2r,\\qquad\n a(x,y)=W_g(x)a(g^{-1}x,g^{-1}y)W_g(y)^*.$$ In particular, if $\\sup_g\\lVert W_g-u_g\\rVert_\\infty\\le\\delta$ for any constant unitaries $u_g$ on a common representation space, the conclusion holds with $\\lVert a-I\\rVert_\\infty\\le4\\delta$ when $2\\delta<1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-diagonal-unitary",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a finite von Neumann algebra with faithful normal tracial state $\\tau$ and separable trace Hilbert space $L^2(A,\\tau)$. Let $W_g\\in L^\\infty(D,\\mathcal U(A))$ be an exact cocycle. Suppose $$\\sup_{g\\in G}\\mathop{\\rm ess\\,sup}_{x,y\\in D}\n \\lVert W_g(x)W_g(y)^*-I\\rVert\\le r<1.$$ Then there is a measurable field $a:D^2\\to\\mathcal U(A)$ such that $$\\label{eq:boundary-diagonal-unitary}\n \\lVert a-I\\rVert_\\infty\\le2r,\\qquad\n a(x,y)=W_g(x)a(g^{-1}x,g^{-1}y)W_g(y)^*.$$ In particular, if $\\sup_g\\lVert W_g-u_g\\rVert_\\infty\\le\\delta$ for any constant unitaries $u_g$ on a common representation space, the conclusion holds with $\\lVert a-I\\rVert_\\infty\\le4\\delta$ when $2\\delta<1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"

      "exact_statement": "Let $A$ be a finite von Neumann algebra with faithful normal tracial state $\\tau$ and separable trace Hilbert space $L^2(A,\\tau)$.         "exact_quote": "For the space $D$ constructed in [lem:boundary-mean](#lem:boundary-mean), let $\\sigma:G\\to\\mathcal U(K)$ be a unitary representation on a separable Hilbert space.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-finite",
        "exact_quote": "There are a number $t_{\\mathrm{fin}}>0$ and a nondecreasing function $\\eta_{\\mathrm{fin}}:(0,t_{\\mathrm{fin}})\\to(0,\\infty)$, with $\\eta_{\\mathrm{fin}}(t)\\to0$ as $t\\downarrow0$, having the following property. Let $H$ be a separable nonzero Hilbert space, let $M\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ be a unital finite factor, and let $N\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ be a von Neumann algebra with the same identity. If $d(M,N)<t<t_{\\mathrm{fin}}$, then $N$ is a finite factor and there is a unitary $u\\in\\mathcal B(H)$ such that $$uMu^*=N,\\qquad \\lVert u-I_H\\rVert\\le\\eta_{\\mathrm{fin}}(t).$$ The threshold and function are independent of $M,N$ and their representations.",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-finite",
          "exact_quote": "There are a number $t_{\\mathrm{fin}}>0$ and a nondecreasing function $\\eta_{\\mathrm{fin}}:(0,t_{\\mathrm{fin}})\\to(0,\\infty)$, with $\\eta_{\\mathrm{fin}}(t)\\to0$ as $t\\downarrow0$, having the following property. Let $H$ be a separable nonzero Hilbert space, let $M\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ be a unital finite factor, and let $N\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ be a von Neumann algebra with the same identity. If $d(M,N)<t<t_{\\mathrm{fin}}$, then $N$ is a finite factor and there is a unitary $u\\in\\mathcal B(H)$ such that $$uMu^*=N,\\qquad \\lVert u-I_H\\rVert\\le\\eta_{\\mathrm{fin}}(t).$$ The threshold and function are independent of $M,N$ and their representations.",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are a number $t_{\\mathrm{fin}}>0$ and a nondecreasing function $\\eta_{\\mathrm{fin}}:(0,t_{\\mathrm{fin}})\\to(0,\\infty)$, with $\\eta_{\\mathrm{fin}}(t)\\to0$ as $t\\downarrow0$, having the following property. Let $H$ be a separable nonzero Hilbert space, let $M\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ be a unital finite factor, and let $N\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ be a von Neumann algebra with the same identity. If $d(M,N)<t<t_{\\mathrm{fin}}$, then $N$ is a finite factor and there is a unitary $u\\in\\mathcal B(H)$ such that $$uMu^*=N,\\qquad \\lVert u-I_H\\rVert\\le\\eta_{\\mathrm{fin}}(t).$$ The threshold and function are independent of $M,N$ and their representations.",
      "explanation": "Uniform stability for finite factors on separable spaces: a nearby algebra is a finite factor and is conjugate by a unitary near the identity, with threshold and modulus independent of the algebras.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-common-position",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M,N$ be separable-predual $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factors acting nondegenerately on one Hilbert space, with mutual strict $g$-near inclusions, where $0<g<1.74\\cdot10^{-13}$. Suppose that an amenable von Neumann subalgebra $R\\subseteq M\\cap N$ satisfies $R'\\cap M\\subseteq R$. There are faithful normal representations $\\pi:M\\to\\mathcal B(K)$ and $\\rho:N\\to\\mathcal B(K)$, a common unit vector $\\Omega$, and a number $\\beta<50948\\sqrt g$ such that:*\n\n1.  *both images are in standard tracial position with vector $\\Omega$;*\n\n2.  *$\\pi|_R=\\rho|_R$, and the images have mutual strict $\\beta$-near inclusions;*\n\n3.  *for original contractions $x\\in M$, $y\\in N$, $$\\lVert\\pi(x)-\\rho(y)\\rVert\\le\\beta+\\lVert x-y\\rVert;$$*\n\n4.  *if $e_R$ is the projection onto $\\overline{\\pi(R)\\Omega}$, then $(\\pi(M)\\cup\\{e_R\\})''=(\\rho(N)\\cup\\{e_R\\})''$.*\n\n*The space $K$ can be taken separable.",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-standard-isomorphism",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $b_0>0$ and $C_{\\mathrm{st}}>0$ such that the following holds. Let $M,N\\subseteq\\mathcal B(K)$ be type $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factors on a separable Hilbert space in common standard tracial position with vector $\\Omega$. Suppose they contain a common hyperfinite $\\mathrm{II}_1$ subfactor $R$, irreducible in $M$ and generated by increasing matrix algebras, and that $J_MrJ_M=J_NrJ_N$ for every $r\\in R$. If $d(M,N)<b<b_0$, then there is a normal unital star-isomorphism $\\vartheta:M\\to N$ satisfying $$\\sup_{x\\in M_1}\\lVert\\vartheta(x)-x\\rVert\\le C_{\\mathrm{st}}b.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-standard-isomorphism",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $b_0>0$ and $C_{\\mathrm{st}}>0$ such that the following holds. Let $M,N\\subseteq\\mathcal B(K)$ be type $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factors on a separable Hilbert space in common standard tracial position with vector $\\Omega$. Suppose they contain a common hyperfinite $\\mathrm{II}_1$ subfactor $R$, irreducible in $M$ and generated by increasing matrix algebras, and that $J_MrJ_M=J_NrJ_N$ for every $r\\in R$. If $d(M,N)<b<b_0$, then there is a normal unital star-isomorphism $\\vartheta:M\\to N$ satisfying $$\\sup_{x\\in M_1}\\lVert\\vartheta(x)-x\\rVert\\le C_{\\mathrm{st}}b.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are absolute constants $b_0>0$ and $C_{\\mathrm{st}}>0$ such that the following holds. Let $M,N\\subseteq\\mathcal B(K)$ be type $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factors on a separable Hilbert space in common standard tracial position with vector $\\Omega$. Suppose they contain a common hyperfinite $\\mathrm{II}_1$ subfactor $R$, irreducible in $M$ and generated by increasing matrix algebras, and that $J_MrJ_M=J_NrJ_N$ for every $r\\in R$. If $d(M,N)<b<b_0$, then there is a normal unital star-isomorphism $\\vartheta:M\\to N$ satisfying $$\\sup_{x\\in M_1}\\lVert\\vartheta(x)-x\\rVert\\le C_{\\mathrm{st}}b.$$",
      "explanation": "In common tracial position, close II_1 factors are joined by a normal isomorphism close to the identity in ordinary norm, built from boundary cocycles and a Jordan map.",

        }

      "statement_anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-standard-isomorphism"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-modular-alignment",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $c_{\\rm mod},C_{\\rm mod}>0$ with the following property. Let $(M,N,\\Omega,R)$ be a prepared pair as in [def:prepared-pair](#def:prepared-pair), acting on the same separable Hilbert space $H$, and suppose that $d_{\\mathrm{cb}}(M,N)<\\gamma<c_{\\rm mod}$. There is a unitary $q\\in\\mathcal B(H)$ such that $$\\lVert q-I\\rVert\\le C_{\\rm mod}\\gamma,\n \\qquad d_{\\mathrm{cb}}(qMq^*,N)\\le C_{\\rm mod}\\gamma,\n \\qquad\n \\Delta_N^{it}(qMq^*)\\Delta_N^{-it}=qMq^*\n \\quad(t\\in\\mathbb R).$$ The algebra $N$ and its modular group are unchanged.",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-modular-alignment",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $c_{\\rm mod},C_{\\rm mod}>0$ with the following property. There is a unitary $q\\in\\mathcal B(H)$ such that $$\\lVert q-I\\rVert\\le C_{\\rm mod}\\gamma,\n \\qquad d_{\\mathrm{cb}}(qMq^*,N)\\le C_{\\rm mod}\\gamma,\n \\qquad\n \\Delta_N^{it}(qMq^*)\\Delta_N^{-it}=qMq^*\n \\quad(t\\in\\mathbb R).$$ The algebra $N$ and its modular group are unchanged.",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are absolute constants $c_{\\rm mod},C_{\\rm mod}>0$ with the following property.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-normalizer-linear",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-normalizer-path",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $e_0,C_{\\rm path}>0$ such that the following holds.         "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-289.1"

      "exact_statement": "There are absolute constants $e_0,C_{\\rm path}>0$ such that the following holds.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-analytic-multiplier",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose multiplication by a bounded field $B(t)$ on $L^2(\\mathbb R,H)$ preserves $P_0\\mathcal K$. Then it is the boundary field of a bounded, operator-norm holomorphic function on the upper half-plane, given by $$\\label{eq:analytic-poisson}\n B(t+ir)=\\int_\\mathbb R\\frac{r}{\\pi((t-s)^2+r^2)}B(s)\\,ds,\n \\qquad r>0.$$ Its norm is at most $\\mathop{\\rm ess\\,sup}_s\\lVert B(s)\\rVert$. For two such fields, and any constant $x\\in\\mathcal B(H)$, the extension of the boundary product $B(s)xS(s)$ is $B(z)xS(z)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-modular-near-normalizers",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M,N\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ be a prepared pair on a separable Hilbert space, with $M$ of type $\\mathrm{III}$ and $d_{\\mathrm{cb}}(M,N)\\leq\\gamma$. Write $$U_P(t)=e^{itL_P},\\qquad L_P=\\log\\Delta_P\\quad(P=M,N),\n \\qquad W(t)=U_N(t)U_M(t)^*.$$ Assume the separated-cut estimate of [cor:modular-cuts](#cor:modular-cuts), with its universal constant, and the local path estimate of [lem:local-normalizer-path](#lem:local-normalizer-path). There are universal constants $\\gamma_0>0$ and $C$ such that, if $0<\\gamma<\\gamma_0$, then for every $t\\in\\mathbb R$ there is a unitary $n_t$ satisfying $$n_tMn_t^*=M,\\qquad \\lVert n_t-W(t)\\rVert\\leq C\\gamma.$$ No regularity of the choices $t\\mapsto n_t$ is asserted or needed.",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-modular-near-normalizers",

There are universal constants $\\gamma_0>0$ and $C$ such that, if $0<\\gamma<\\gamma_0$, then for every $t\\in\\mathbb R$ there is a unitary $n_t$ satisfying $$n_tMn_t^*=M,\\qquad \\lVert n_t-W(t)\\rVert\\leq C\\gamma.$$ No regularity of the choices $t\\mapsto n_t$ is asserted or needed.",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"

      "exact_statement": "Let $M,N\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ be a prepared pair on a separable Hilbert space, with $M$ of type $\\mathrm{III}$ and $d_{\\mathrm{cb}}(M,N)\\leq\\gamma$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-core-fixed-points",
        "exact_quote": "With this notation, $$\\label{eq:core-fixed-description}\n C(P,U)=\n \\bigl(P\\mathbin{\\overline\\otimes}\\mathcal B(L^2(\\mathbb R))\\bigr)^\\alpha,\n \\qquad\n \\alpha_s=\\operatorname{Ad}(U_s\\otimes\\rho_s).$$ For $(Q_a\\xi)(t)=e^{iat}\\xi(t)$, character conjugation $\\beta_a=\\operatorname{Ad}(Q_a)$ preserves $C(P,U)$, and $$\\label{eq:core-character-fixed}\n C(P,U)^\\beta=\\pi(P).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-289.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-core-fixed-points",
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Kadison–Kastler distance** ([source](#eq:kk-definition)): The Kadison–Kastler distance between two such algebras is $$\label{eq:kk-definition} d(M,N)=\max\left\{ \sup_{x\in M_1}\inf_{y\in N_1}\lVert x-y\rVert,\quad \sup_{y\in N_1}\inf_{x\in M_1}\lVert x-y\rVert\right\}.$$
- **Unit ball notation** ([source](#eq:kk-definition)): For a von Neumann algebra $M\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$, write $M_1$ for its closed operator-norm unit ball.
- **Completely bounded distance** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For concretely represented von Neumann algebras define $$d_{\mathrm{cb}}(A,B)=\sup_{n\ge1}d(M_n(A),M_n(B)).$$
- **Completely bounded norm** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For a linear map $T:A\to\mathcal B(H)$, let $T_n$ act entrywise on $M_n(A)$, and put $\lVert T\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}=\sup_n\lVert T_n\rVert$.
- **Near inclusion** ([source](#pm-h-6)): We write $A\subset_\gamma B$ if there is $\gamma'<\gamma$ such that every $x\in A_1$ has $\operatorname{dist}(x,B)\le\gamma'$; the approximant in $B$ need not be contractive.
- **Unitary group, commutant and center notation** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For $A\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$, write $\mathcal U(A)$ for its unitary group, $A'=\{T\in\mathcal B(H):Ta=aT\text{ for every }a\in A\}$ for its commutant, and $Z(A)=A\cap A'$ for its center.
- **Unital completely positive map** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A map is *unital completely positive*, abbreviated UCP, if it preserves the identity and each of its matrix amplifications is positive.
- **Inner derivation** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For $h\in\mathcal B(H)$ write $\operatorname{ad}_h(x)=[h,x]=hx-xh$.
- **Column norm** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For $a_1,\ldots,a_l\in\mathcal B(H)$, write $\lVert(a_i)_i\rVert_{\mathrm{col}}=\lVert\sum_i a_i^*a_i\rVert^{1/2}$.
- **Haar unitary** ([source](#pm-h-17)): A unitary $s$ is *Haar* if $\tau(s^m)=0$ for every nonzero $m\in\mathbb Z$.
- **Scalar freeness** ([source](#pm-h-17)): Two unital subalgebras are *scalar free* if every alternating product of trace-zero elements from them has trace zero.
- **Irreducible inclusion** ([source](#pm-h-27)): An inclusion $R\subseteq M$ is *irreducible* when $R'\cap M=\mathbb CI$.
- **Standard tracial position** ([source](#pm-h-28)): A factor $P\subseteq\mathcal B(K)$ is in *standard tracial position* with unit vector $\Omega$ when $x\mapsto x\Omega$ identifies $K$ with $L^2(P,\tau_P)$.
- **Centralizer of a state** ([source](#pm-h-32)): For a faithful normal state $\psi$ on an algebra $P$, its *centralizer* is $$P_\psi=\{a\in P:\psi(ax)=\psi(xa)\text{ for every }x\in P\}.$$
- **Bicentralizer** ([source](#pm-h-32)): $$ The generated subgroup is countable and dense in $\mathbb R$. Test the field on countably many vector coefficients. Translation continuity in local $L^1$ extends its invariance to every real translation, and convolution with compactly supported approximate identities shows that each coefficient is constant. Thus $Z=a_0\otimes1$ is a constant field. Commutation with $\pi(A\otimes1)$, using strong continuity of the modular action and a countable strongly dense set, gives $a_0\in A'\cap A=\mathbb C1$. The core is a factor, so the continuous-core criterion makes $P$ a type $\mathrm{III}_1$ factor. Finally, adjoining another countable family of $\lambda$-sites and reindexing that family gives the asserted absorption. All these tensor products preserve the displayed product states. The usual identification of the two-ratio product with the hyperfinite $\mathrm{III}_1$ factor is also recorded in (Haagerup 1987, Example 1.6, p. 103) [[Haagerup1987](#ref-Haagerup1987)]; the argument above establishes the additional assertion for every $A$. ◻ </details> For a faithful normal state $\psi$ on $P$, its *bicentralizer* $\mathrm{B}(P,\psi)$ consists of those $a\in P$ for which $\lVert[a,x_n]\rVert_\psi\to0$ whenever $(x_n)$ is a bounded sequence in $P$ and $\lVert x_n\psi-\psi x_n\rVert\to0$.
- **Prepared pair** ([source](#stmt-def-prepared-pair)): A *prepared pair* consists of type $\mathrm{III}_1$ factors $M,N\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ on a separable Hilbert space, a common cyclic separating unit vector $\Omega$, and a hyperfinite $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $R\subseteq M\cap N$ such that 1. $R'\cap M=R'\cap N=\mathbb C1$; 2. the vector state $\varphi(x)=\langle x\Omega,\Omega\rangle$ centralizes $R$ when restricted to either algebra; 3. $J_MrJ_M=J_NrJ_N$ for every $r\in R$, where $J_M,J_N$ are the modular conjugations associated with $\Omega$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-amenable-displacement | displacement of the unitary moving an amenable algebra into a near neighbor | ≤ | $150\gamma$ | near-inclusion parameter below the stated threshold |
| q-amenable-equality-threshold | two-sided threshold giving exact conjugacy of amenable algebras | < | $1/101$ | mutual near inclusions |
| q-commutant-gap | distance between commutants | ≤ | $4\gamma$ | cb gap at most gamma |
| q-paired-displacement | displacement of the intertwining unitary for close representations | ≤ | $4e$ | cb distance below the threshold |
| q-ucp-displacement | displacement of the implementing unitary in the UCP criterion | ≤ | $128e$ | cb error e below the stated small threshold |
| q-ucp-isomorphism | cb distance between the isomorphism and the representation | ≤ | $32e$ | cb error e below the stated small threshold |
| q-column-constant | column commutator constant | ≤ | $8\delta$ | delta is the ordinary derivation norm |
| q-free-word-distinct-bound | norm bound for projection-word sums with distinct labels | ≤ | $7\sqrt{(r-1)!}\,d^{-(r-1)/2}$ | dimension at least the threshold depending on r |
| q-through-label-sum | sum of squared word bounds through one label | ≤ | $64$ | all lengths up to the cutoff |
| q-amplification-constant | explicit admissible amplification constant | = | $600$ | all matrix levels |
| q-positive-scalar | lower bound on the expected scalar | ≥ | $\frac12$ | independent of the matrix size |
| q-cocycle-defect-threshold | admissible defect of an approximate cocycle | ≤ | $\frac1{64}$ | countable group |
| q-cocycle-correction | distance from the exact cocycle to the approximate one | ≤ | $2e$ | defect at most the threshold |
| q-finite-neighbor-threshold | gap ensuring a neighbor of a finite factor is a finite factor | < | $1/10$ | Kadison–Kastler distance |
| q-common-position-threshold | near-inclusion threshold for common tracial position | < | $1.74\cdot10^{-13}$ | mutual strict near inclusions |
| q-common-position-beta | near-inclusion parameter in common position | < | $50948\sqrt g$ | g below the threshold |
| q-ball-test-constant | constant in testing a bimodular operator on unit-ball elements | ≤ | $384$ | absolute |
| q-conjugation-constant | Lipschitz constant of the modular conjugations | = | $768$ | absolute |
| q-center-distance | distance between centers | ≤ | $3d(M,N)$ | absolute |
| q-center-alignment-displacement | displacement of the unitary aligning the centers | ≤ | $450g$ | g below the stated threshold |
