<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-amenable** [claim](#stmt-thm-amenable) / [proof](#stmt-thm-amenable): Cited near-inclusion theorem of Christensen type, quoted with its strict convention. -> claim-amenable-near-inclusion
- **s-commutant-cb** [claim](#stmt-lem-commutant-cb) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-commutant-cb): Preannihilator and singular-value decomposition of trace-class functionals, then a reducing projection with entries in A. -> claim-commutant-cb
- **s-paired** [claim](#stmt-lem-paired) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-paired): Switch operator on the direct sum, commutant lemma, then polar part of the lower off-diagonal block. -> claim-close-representations
- **s-ucp** [claim](#stmt-prop-ucp) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ucp): Surjectivity of the UCP map, transported product, multiplication perturbation theorem, then the close-representations lemma. -> claim-ucp-criterion
- **s-tensor-gap** [claim](#stmt-lem-tensor-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tensor-gap): Compression by finite-rank projections, weak cluster points, and averaging over unitary groups of finite stages. -> claim-tensor-gap
- **s-invariant-mean** [claim](#stmt-lem-invariant-mean) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-invariant-mean): Coefficientwise application of the scalar mean to quadratic forms. -> claim-invariant-mean
- **s-column** [claim](#stmt-lem-column-commutator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-column-commutator): Triangular homomorphism of a derivation and Haagerup's cyclic similarity theorem. -> claim-column-estimate
- **s-trace-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-trace-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trace-transfer): Density of trace functionals in the predual by Hahn–Banach. -> claim-trace-transfer
- **s-free-words** [claim](#stmt-lem-free-words) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-free-words): Fock-space model of reduced words, cut decomposition into creation, preservation and annihilation, tuple-matrix norms, scalar remainder. -> claim-free-words
- **s-through-label** [claim](#stmt-cor-through-label) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-through-label): Count distinct-label and repeated-label multisets containing a label and sum squared bounds. -> claim-through-label
- **s-free-lifts** [claim](#stmt-lem-free-lifts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-free-lifts): Popa's independence theorem in a matrix model, conjugation by a Haar unitary for freeness from the constant algebra, lifts of ultraproduct unitaries. -> claim-free-lifts
- **s-haar-twist** [claim](#stmt-lem-haar-twist) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-haar-twist): Cyclic mixed trace words and Fejér approximation. -> claim-haar-twist
- **s-amplification** [claim](#stmt-thm-amplification) / [proof](#pm-h-22): Random phases on diagonal projections, spectral truncation, column estimate, recovery of a positive scalar weak limit, extraction of the matrix commutator. -> claim-uniform-amplification
- **s-boundary-mean** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-mean) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-mean): Random-walk path space, shift-invariant sigma algebra, weak-star cluster of Cesàro averages, coefficientwise extension to operator fields. -> claim-boundary-mean
- **s-boundary-correction** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-correction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-correction): Quadratic averaging and polar correction iteration. -> claim-boundary-correction
- **s-boundary-intertwiner** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-intertwiner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-intertwiner): Average the product of the cocycle with the adjoint representation, then take the polar part. -> claim-boundary-intertwiner
- **s-boundary-diagonal** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-diagonal-unitary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-diagonal-unitary): Least-norm vector in a closed convex hull in the trace Hilbert space, measurability by rational combinations, polar part. -> claim-boundary-diagonal-unitary
- **s-double-ergodicity** [claim](#stmt-lem-double-ergodicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-double-ergodicity): Bilateral random-walk coordinates, independence of blocks, intersection of independent sigma algebras. -> claim-double-ergodicity
- **s-finite-neighbor** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-neighbor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-neighbor): Exclude proper isometries by polar approximation, exclude central projections by tracial conjugacy averaging. -> claim-finite-neighbor
- **s-irreducible-hyperfinite** [claim](#stmt-lem-irreducible-hyperfinite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-irreducible-hyperfinite): Inductive choice of commuting matrix algebras making each element nearly scalar under averaging. -> claim-irreducible-hyperfinite
- **s-common-position** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-common-position) / [proof](#stmt-prop-finite-common-position): Cited common-position construction placing both factors on one tracial Hilbert space. -> claim-common-position
- **s-finite-right-action** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-right-action) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-right-action): Identify the commutant of the generated algebra with the right action and use equality of basic constructions. -> claim-finite-right-action
- **s-ball-tests** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-ball-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-ball-tests): Haar averaging by unitaries of the subfactor, random signs, fourth-moment bound and spectral truncation. -> claim-finite-ball-tests
- **s-tracial-conjugations** [claim](#stmt-lem-tracial-conjugations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tracial-conjugations): Apply the ball-test lemma to the difference of the two conjugations. -> claim-tracial-conjugations
- **s-jordan-orientation** [claim](#stmt-lem-jordan-orientation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-jordan-orientation): Polarized triple identity, primeness of a factor, two-subgroup argument. -> claim-jordan-orientation
- **s-finite-standard-isomorphism** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-standard-isomorphism) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-standard-isomorphism): Boundary cocycle, averaged intertwiner, diagonal unitary, double ergodicity, extension to a Jordan isomorphism and exclusion of the antimultiplicative case. -> claim-finite-standard-isomorphism
- **s-finite** [claim](#stmt-thm-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-standard-isomorphism-2): Align a common subfactor, pass to common position, apply the standard isomorphism, then convert ordinary to cb control by amplification and implement by the close-representations lemma. -> claim-finite-factors
- **s-common-infinite** [claim](#stmt-lem-common-infinite-amplification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-common-infinite-amplification): Identify matrix amplifications with the algebras themselves using common isometries. -> claim-common-infinite-amplification
- **s-fixed-tensor** [claim](#stmt-lem-fixed-tensor-factor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fixed-tensor-factor): Site swaps for trivial relative commutant, frequency invariance of central fields in the modular crossed product, absorption by reindexing sites. -> claim-fixed-tensor-factor
- **s-prepared-pair** [claim](#stmt-prop-prepared-pair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-prepared-pair): Common copy of the bounded operators on the square-summable sequences, tensoring with the fixed factor, bicentralizer and expected subfactor, two successive small changes of position. -> claim-prepared-pair-existence
- **s-modular-graphs** [claim](#stmt-prop-modular-graphs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-modular-graphs): Random signs and unitaries to flatten a graph vector, clip, replicate into a row so cb closeness applies, then rescale. -> claim-modular-graphs
- **s-modular-cuts** [claim](#stmt-cor-modular-cuts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-modular-cuts): Resolvent comparison from graph projections and an integral intertwining formula for separated spectra. -> claim-modular-cuts
- **s-infinite-commutant** [claim](#stmt-lem-infinite-commutant-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-infinite-commutant-bound): Average over finite-dimensional unital algebras in a copy of the bounded operators, then reduce to the cb commutant lemma. -> claim-infinite-commutant-bound
- **s-normalizer-linear** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-normalizer-linear) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-normalizer-linear): Contradiction with norm ultraproducts, simplicity of the ultraproduct and Sakai's derivation theorem. -> claim-normalizer-linear
- **s-normalizer-path** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-normalizer-path) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-normalizer-path): Continuity barrier argument using the linear estimate and the logarithm. -> claim-normalizer-path
- **s-analytic-multiplier** [claim](#stmt-lem-analytic-multiplier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-analytic-multiplier): Cayley transform to the circle, Fourier support and Poisson integral. -> claim-analytic-multiplier
- **s-modular-near-normalizers** [claim](#stmt-lem-modular-near-normalizers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-modular-near-normalizers): Arveson distance formula on the frequency nest, character averaging, analytic extension, polar smoothing, local path estimate, UCP limit and the UCP criterion. -> claim-modular-near-normalizers
- **s-small-exact-normalizer** [claim](#stmt-lem-small-exact-normalizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-small-exact-normalizer): Principal logarithm, derivation of the factor, Sakai's theorem and spectral midpoint shift. -> claim-small-exact-normalizer
- **s-normalizer-representation-correction** [claim](#stmt-lem-normalizer-representation-correction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-normalizer-representation-correction): Invariant-mean averaging with polar correction kept inside the normalizer group by the local factorization. -> claim-normalizer-representation-correction
- **s-modular-alignment** [claim](#stmt-prop-modular-alignment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-normalizer-representation-correction-2): Pointwise normalizers, exact representation, averaged intertwiner and polar unitary giving the change of position. -> claim-modular-alignment
- **s-core-fixed-points** [claim](#stmt-lem-core-fixed-points) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-core-fixed-points): Smoothed kernels, covariance, masked reconstruction, translation-invariant fields are constant. -> claim-core-fixed-points
- **s-core-gap** [claim](#stmt-lem-core-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-core-gap): Tensor gap lemma followed by averaging over the common action. -> claim-core-gap
- **s-core-descent** [claim](#stmt-prop-core-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-core-descent): Matrix units in the semifinite factor, align a copy of the bounded operators, finite-corner theorem, inflate, average over characters and apply the UCP criterion. -> claim-core-descent
- **s-factors** [claim](#stmt-thm-factors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-factors): Finite case directly; infinite case through preparation, modular alignment, core descent and a slice map followed by the UCP criterion. -> claim-separable-factors
- **s-centers-close** [claim](#stmt-lem-centers-close) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-centers-close): Dixmier averaging for the center gap, then the amenable near-inclusion theorem. -> claim-centers-close
- **s-fiber-gap** [claim](#stmt-lem-fiber-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fiber-gap): Countable dense families, quotient lifts and weak cluster points on a conull set. -> claim-fiber-gap
- **s-measurable-implementers** [claim](#stmt-lem-measurable-implementers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-measurable-implementers): Borel relation on a Polish unitary group and measurable selection by uniformization. -> claim-measurable-implementers
- **s-separable-algebras** [claim](#stmt-thm-separable-algebras) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-separable-algebras): Align centers, apply the factor theorem on fibers, assemble measurably. -> claim-separable-algebras
- **s-separable-tests** [claim](#stmt-lem-separable-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separable-tests): Countable closing-off construction of separable algebras and a reducing subspace, then transfer of the gap through restriction and Kaplansky density. -> claim-separable-tests
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separable-tests-2): Net of separable local conjugacies, weak-operator cluster UCP map with range in the neighbor, then the UCP criterion. -> claim-main
- **s-uniform-matrix-commutant** [claim](#stmt-cor-uniform-matrix-commutant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-uniform-matrix-commutant): Conjugate by the diagonal amplification of the unitary from the main theorem. -> claim-uniform-matrix-commutant

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-mean",
          "exact_quote": "For every countable group $G$ there are a standard probability space $(D,\\nu)$ with a nonsingular $G$-action and a positive unital contraction $$m:L^\\infty(G\\times D)\\longrightarrow L^\\infty(D)$$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:boundary-mean-identities}\n m(\\lambda_g F)=\\alpha_g(mF),\\qquad m(fF)=f\\,m(F)\n \\quad(f\\in L^\\infty(D)).$$ There is a mean $m^{(2)}$ with the same properties for the separate $G\\times G$-action on $D\\times D$ and functions on $G^2\\times D^2$. Each mean extends to a contraction on bounded operator fields on any separable $K$. The extension preserves adjoints and von Neumann algebra values, and is a bimodule map over bounded operator fields independent of the group variable: $$\\label{eq:boundary-operator-module}\n m(BFC)=B\\,m(F)\\,C.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "s-boundary-mean",
      "inputs": [],
      "method": "Random-walk path space, shift-invariant sigma algebra, weak-star cluster of Cesàro averages, coefficientwise extension to operator fields.",
      "output": "claim-boundary-mean",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-boundary-mean"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-correction",
      "depends_on": [
        "s-boundary-mean"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-correction",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D$ and $m$ be as in [lem:boundary-mean](#lem:boundary-mean), and let $A\\subset\\mathcal B(K)$ be a von Neumann algebra on a separable Hilbert space. Suppose $V_g\\in L^\\infty(D,\\mathcal U(A))$, $g\\in G$, and $$e=\\sup_{g,h\\in G}\\lVert V_g\\alpha_g(V_h)-V_{gh}\\rVert_\\infty\n \\le\\frac1{64}.$$ Then there are measurable unitary fields $W_g$ with $$\\label{eq:boundary-exact-cocycle}\n W_g\\alpha_g(W_h)=W_{gh},\\qquad\n \\sup_g\\lVert W_g-V_g\\rVert_\\infty\\le 2e.$$ In particular $W_1=I$. The identities hold simultaneously almost everywhere for every $g,h$, and may be used on a $G$-invariant conull subset of $D$.",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "s-boundary-correction",
      "inputs": [
        "claim-boundary-mean"
      ],
      "method": "Quadratic averaging and polar correction iteration.",
      "output": "claim-boundary-correction",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-boundary-correction"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-intertwiner",
      "depends_on": [
        "s-boundary-mean"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-intertwiner",
          "exact_quote": "Take either $(\\Gamma,Z)=(G,D)$ or $(\\Gamma,Z)=(G^2,D^2)$, with its mean from [lem:boundary-mean](#lem:boundary-mean). Let $U:\\Gamma\\to\\mathcal U(K)$ be a unitary representation on a separable Hilbert space, and let $\\Sigma_s\\in L^\\infty(Z,\\mathcal U(K))$ be an exact cocycle for the given action. If $$\\sup_{s\\in\\Gamma}\\lVert\\Sigma_s-U_s\\rVert_\\infty\\le\\delta<1,$$ there is $L\\in L^\\infty(Z,\\mathcal U(K))$ such that $$\\label{eq:boundary-intertwiner}\n \\lVert L-I\\rVert_\\infty\\le2\\delta,\\qquad\n L(z)U_s=\\Sigma_s(z)L(s^{-1}z).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "s-boundary-intertwiner",
      "inputs": [
        "claim-boundary-mean"
      ],
      "method": "Average the product of the cocycle with the adjoint representation, then take the polar part.",
      "output": "claim-boundary-intertwiner",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-boundary-intertwiner"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-diagonal-unitary",
      "depends_on": [
        "s-boundary-mean"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-diagonal-unitary",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a finite von Neumann algebra with faithful normal tracial state $\\tau$ and separable trace Hilbert space $L^2(A,\\tau)$. Let $W_g\\in L^\\infty(D,\\mathcal U(A))$ be an exact cocycle. Suppose $$\\sup_{g\\in G}\\mathop{\\rm ess\\,sup}_{x,y\\in D}\n \\lVert W_g(x)W_g(y)^*-I\\rVert\\le r<1.$$ Then there is a measurable field $a:D^2\\to\\mathcal U(A)$ such that $$\\label{eq:boundary-diagonal-unitary}\n \\lVert a-I\\rVert_\\infty\\le2r,\\qquad\n a(x,y)=W_g(x)a(g^{-1}x,g^{-1}y)W_g(y)^*.$$ In particular, if $\\sup_g\\lVert W_g-u_g\\rVert_\\infty\\le\\delta$ for any constant unitaries $u_g$ on a common representation space, the conclusion holds with $\\lVert a-I\\rVert_\\infty\\le4\\delta$ when $2\\delta<1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "s-boundary-diagonal",
      "inputs": [
        "claim-boundary-mean"
      ],
      "method": "Least-norm vector in a closed convex hull in the trace Hilbert space, measurability by rational combinations, polar part.",
      "output": "claim-boundary-diagonal-unitary",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-boundary-diagonal-unitary"
    },

          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-common-position",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-common-position",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M,N$ be separable-predual $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factors acting nondegenerately on one Hilbert space, with mutual strict $g$-near inclusions, where $0<g<1.74\\cdot10^{-13}$. Suppose that an amenable von Neumann subalgebra $R\\subseteq M\\cap N$ satisfies $R'\\cap M\\subseteq R$. There are faithful normal representations $\\pi:M\\to\\mathcal B(K)$ and $\\rho:N\\to\\mathcal B(K)$, a common unit vector $\\Omega$, and a number $\\beta<50948\\sqrt g$ such that:*\n\n1.  *both images are in standard tracial position with vector $\\Omega$;*\n\n2.  *$\\pi|_R=\\rho|_R$, and the images have mutual strict $\\beta$-near inclusions;*\n\n3.  *for original contractions $x\\in M$, $y\\in N$, $$\\lVert\\pi(x)-\\rho(y)\\rVert\\le\\beta+\\lVert x-y\\rVert;$$*\n\n4.  *if $e_R$ is the projection onto $\\overline{\\pi(R)\\Omega}$, then $(\\pi(M)\\cup\\{e_R\\})''=(\\rho(N)\\cup\\{e_R\\})''$.*\n\n*The space $K$ can be taken separable.",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "s-common-position",
      "inputs": [],
      "method": "Cited common-position construction placing both factors on one tracial Hilbert space.",
      "output": "claim-common-position",
      "proof_anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-common-position"
    },

          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "s-finite-right-action",

If $d(M,N)<b<b_0$, then there is a normal unital star-isomorphism $\\vartheta:M\\to N$ satisfying $$\\sup_{x\\in M_1}\\lVert\\vartheta(x)-x\\rVert\\le C_{\\mathrm{st}}b.$$",

        }

Let $H$ be a separable nonzero Hilbert space, let $M\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ be a unital finite factor, and let $N\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ be a von Neumann algebra with the same identity. If $d(M,N)<t<t_{\\mathrm{fin}}$, then $N$ is a finite factor and there is a unitary $u\\in\\mathcal B(H)$ such that $$uMu^*=N,\\qquad \\lVert u-I_H\\rVert\\le\\eta_{\\mathrm{fin}}(t).$$ The threshold and function are independent of $M,N$ and their representations.",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }
      ],

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-analytic-multiplier",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose multiplication by a bounded field $B(t)$ on $L^2(\\mathbb R,H)$ preserves $P_0\\mathcal K$. Then it is the boundary field of a bounded, operator-norm holomorphic function on the upper half-plane, given by $$\\label{eq:analytic-poisson}\n B(t+ir)=\\int_\\mathbb R\\frac{r}{\\pi((t-s)^2+r^2)}B(s)\\,ds,\n \\qquad r>0.$$ Its norm is at most $\\mathop{\\rm ess\\,sup}_s\\lVert B(s)\\rVert$. For two such fields, and any constant $x\\in\\mathcal B(H)$, the extension of the boundary product $B(s)xS(s)$ is $B(z)xS(z)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-289.1"
        }

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-uniform-tolerance**: The tolerance delta is a function of epsilon alone: it does not depend on the algebras, their representations or the Hilbert space. Within delta, the conjugating unitary lies within epsilon of the identity.
- **inv-amenable-near-inclusion**: A one-sided near inclusion of an amenable algebra into another algebra is realised by a unitary with displacement linear in the near-inclusion parameter, located in the algebra generated by both.
- **inv-ucp-criterion**: A unital completely positive map close in completely bounded norm to a faithful normal representation, with surjectivity from the reverse ordinary gap, yields a normal isomorphism and a spatial unitary with displacement linear in the error; the map need not be normal.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-one-sided-nonamenable**: Applying the near-inclusion unitary to a non-amenable algebra that is only one-sidedly close to another. -> The near-inclusion theorem gives no small unitary without amenability of the included algebra; the conclusion is not guaranteed.
- **anti-mismatched-identity**: Using the main stability theorem for algebras that do not share the same identity or are not unital. -> The hypotheses are not met, so no unitary of small displacement is guaranteed.
- **anti-ucp-without-surjectivity**: Using the completely positive criterion on a map whose range is not known to be near the target algebra. -> Without the reverse gap bound the map need not be onto and no isomorphism follows.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper's results are existence theorems on operator algebras over arbitrary, possibly infinite-dimensional, Hilbert spaces: the main theorem asserts that a unitary exists. Its proof uses non-constructive tools (ultrafilters, weak-operator cluster points, invariant means, measurable selection). There is no finite procedure with explicit steps, so no runnable reference implementation or exhaustive oracle exists.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-common-identity-unital** (hypothesis): The main stability statement is for unital von Neumann algebras with the same identity on one complex Hilbert space; algebras with different identities, non-unital algebras or algebras on different spaces are outside it. Blocked use: ['Applying the stability theorem to algebras whose identities differ', 'Applying it to non-unital algebras or to algebras acting on different Hilbert spaces']
- **limit-weak-closure** (formal_scope): The conclusion concerns von Neumann algebras only. The introduction treats norm-closed C*-algebras separately and notes that examples exclude a uniform displacement bound for them. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem for norm-closed C*-algebras that are not weakly closed', 'Assuming spatial conjugacy by a near-identity unitary for merely norm-separable algebras']
- **limit-amenable-near-inclusion** (hypothesis): The near-inclusion theorem needs the included algebra to be amenable and the near-inclusion parameter to be below the stated thresholds; exact equality needs the mutual near inclusion. Blocked use: ['Moving a non-amenable algebra into a neighbour by a small unitary using this theorem', 'Using the theorem when the near-inclusion parameter exceeds the stated bound']
- **limit-ucp-smallness** (hypothesis): The completely positive criterion requires a faithful normal unital representation, a small cb error, a bound on the reverse ordinary gap and the sum of gap and error below one. Blocked use: ['Invoking the criterion on a map whose range is not known to be near the target algebra', 'Invoking it when the cb error exceeds the stated threshold']
- **limit-amplification-factor-type** (hypothesis): The amplification estimate is proved for separably acting type II_1 factors and selfadjoint implementers; its constant is absolute. Blocked use: ['Using the amplification constant for non-factor or non-separably acting algebras without a separate argument', 'Using it for non-selfadjoint implementers directly']
- **limit-nonconstructive-unitary** (nonconstructive): The conjugating unitary is obtained from subnets, weak-operator cluster points and measurable selection, so the paper gives no procedure that computes it. Blocked use: ['Presenting the result as an algorithm that outputs the unitary', 'Quoting a running time for finding the unitary']
- **limit-finite-threshold** (approximation): The finite-factor route uses a very small near-inclusion threshold, so explicit constants there are valid only in a tiny neighbourhood. Blocked use: ['Assuming the explicit common-position constants hold at moderate distances']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 289 (`lean/docs/289.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Universal strong Kadison–Kastler stability](../../preprints/Universal-strong-Kadison-Kastler-stability-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves the strong Kadison–Kastler conjecture uniformly. For every $\varepsilon>0$, there is a $\delta>0$ such that any two unital von Neumann algebras on the same complex Hilbert space at Kadison–Kastler distance below $\delta$ are conjugate by a unitary $u$ with $\lVert u-1\rVert<\varepsilon$. The tolerance depends only on $\varepsilon$, uniformly over the algebras, their representations, and the Hilbert space.

Comparator source `StrongKadisonKastler.lean` (1386 bytes, sha256 `a3e28c6071cd68977a43738c8aed6f22f78b1ba14b3b7f6c76e1a570861b3f93`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-! Theorem 1.1 of *Universal strong Kadison–Kastler stability*:
one tolerance gives small unitary conjugacies on every complex Hilbert space. -/

namespace KadisonKastler

noncomputable section

universe u

variable {H : Type u} [NormedAddCommGroup H] [InnerProductSpace ℂ H] [CompleteSpace H]

def unitBall (M : VonNeumannAlgebra H) : Set (H →L[ℂ] H) :=
  {x | x ∈ M ∧ ‖x‖ ≤ 1}

noncomputable def kkDistance (M N : VonNeumannAlgebra H) : ℝ :=
  Metric.hausdorffDist (unitBall M) (unitBall N)

def unitaryConj (v : unitary (H →L[ℂ] H)) (x : H →L[ℂ] H) : H →L[ℂ] H :=
  (v : H →L[ℂ] H) * x * star (v : H →L[ℂ] H)

def Conjugates (v : unitary (H →L[ℂ] H)) (M N : VonNeumannAlgebra H) : Prop :=
  unitaryConj v '' (M : Set (H →L[ℂ] H)) = (N : Set (H →L[ℂ] H))

def NearConjugacy (M N : VonNeumannAlgebra H) (ε : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∃ v : unitary (H →L[ℂ] H), Conjugates v M N ∧ ‖(v : H →L[ℂ] H) - 1‖ < ε

def UniversalStrongStability : Prop :=
  ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → ∃ δ : ℝ, 0 < δ ∧
    ∀ (H : Type u) [NormedAddCommGroup H] [InnerProductSpace ℂ H] [CompleteSpace H],
      ∀ M N : VonNeumannAlgebra H,
        kkDistance M N < δ → NearConjugacy M N ε

theorem universal_strong_stability : UniversalStrongStability.{u} := by
  sorry

end

end KadisonKastler

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that sufficiently close unital von Neumann algebras are conjugate by a unitary arbitrarily close to the identity. The tolerance depends only on the prescribed distance of that unitary from the identity, uniformly over all algebras, representations, and Hilbert spaces. This resolves the strong Kadison–Kastler conjecture.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

<a id="eq:kk-definition"></a>
For a von Neumann algebra $M\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$, write $M_1$ for its closed operator-norm unit ball. The Kadison–Kastler distance between two such algebras is $$\label{eq:kk-definition}
 d(M,N)=\max\left\{
 \sup_{x\in M_1}\inf_{y\in N_1}\lVert x-y\rVert,\quad
 \sup_{y\in N_1}\inf_{x\in M_1}\lVert x-y\rVert\right\}.$$ Kadison and Kastler introduced this metric in 1972 (Kadison and Kastler 1972) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[KadisonKastler1972](#ref-KadisonKastler1972)]. Their perturbation problem asks whether closeness of the unit balls forces spatial isomorphism, implemented by a unitary close to the identity. We prove the uniform form of this assertion.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Strong Kadison–Kastler stability). *For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $\delta=\delta(\varepsilon)>0$ such that the following holds. If $H$ is any complex Hilbert space and $M,N\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ are unital von Neumann algebras with identity $I_H$ and $d(M,N)<\delta$, there is a unitary $u\in\mathcal B(H)$ satisfying $$uMu^*=N,\qquad \lVert u-I_H\rVert<\varepsilon.$$*

</div>

The tolerance is independent of the algebras, their type, their representations, and the cardinalities of their preduals and Hilbert spaces. Thus [thm:main](#thm:main) resolves the strong Kadison–Kastler conjecture positively. Its hypothesis concerns the ordinary distance <a href="#eq:kk-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:kk-definition">[eq:kk-definition]</a>.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Historical context

Early positive results treated type I algebras: Phillips proved spatial isomorphism, and Christensen obtained a conjugating unitary close to the identity (Phillips 1974; Christensen 1975) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Phillips1974](#ref-Phillips1974), [Christensen1975](#ref-Christensen1975)]. The work of Christensen, Johnson, and Raeburn–Taylor established the amenable case (Christensen 1977a; Johnson 1977; Raeburn and Taylor 1977) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Christensen1977](#ref-Christensen1977), [Johnson1977](#ref-Johnson1977), [RaeburnTaylor1977](#ref-RaeburnTaylor1977)]. Christensen subsequently developed the one-sided theory of near inclusions: an amenable von Neumann algebra sufficiently nearly contained in another can be moved into it by a small unitary (Christensen 1980) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Christensen1980](#ref-Christensen1980)]. His earlier work also treated close von Neumann subalgebras of a common finite von Neumann algebra (Christensen 1977b) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Christensen1977II](#ref-Christensen1977II)]. The common finite ambient algebra is an essential hypothesis of that result; merely assuming that both represented algebras are finite gives a different problem. In a different common-ambient setting, Ino proved near-identity unitary conjugacy for sufficiently close subalgebras of a countably decomposable ambient algebra when both admit conditional expectations of finite probabilistic index from that algebra (Ino 2016, Theorem 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Ino2016](#ref-Ino2016)].

The corresponding development for $C^*$-algebras clarifies the role of the conclusion. Christensen, Sinclair, Smith, White, and Winter proved that sufficiently close $C^*$-algebras acting on a separable Hilbert space are spatially isomorphic if one of them is norm separable and nuclear (Christensen, Sinclair, Smith, White, and Winter 2010, 2012) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[CSSWW2010](#ref-CSSWW2010), [CSSWW2012](#ref-CSSWW2012)]. Johnson’s examples exclude a uniform bound on the displacement of an implementing unitary that tends to zero with the algebra distance; they do not exclude spatial isomorphism (Johnson 1982) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Johnson1982](#ref-Johnson1982)].

For nonamenable von Neumann algebras, Cameron, Christensen, Sinclair, Smith, White, and Wiggins constructed strongly stable factors of the form $(L^\infty(X)\rtimes\mathrm{SL}_n(\mathbb Z))\mathbin{\overline\otimes}R$, where $n\geq3$, the action is free, ergodic, and probability preserving on a standard nonatomic probability space, and $R$ is the hyperfinite type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor (Cameron et al. 2012, 2014) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[CCSSWW2012](#ref-CCSSWW2012), [CCSSWW2014](#ref-CCSSWW2014)]. Their proof combines amenable near-inclusion methods, a common standard representation, and bounded cohomology. Their later work transfers property $\Gamma$ and Cartan structure between sufficiently close type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors with separable predual, and also studies stability of solidity, strong solidity, and tensor-product structure (Cameron et al. 2017) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[CCSSWW2017](#ref-CCSSWW2017)].

The passage between representations is tied to Kadison’s similarity problem: whether a bounded homomorphism from a $C^*$-algebra into $\mathcal B(H)$ is conjugate by an invertible operator to a $*$-homomorphism. Christensen’s work on property $\Gamma$ factors provides a foundational special case (Christensen 1986, 2001) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Christensen1986](#ref-Christensen1986), [Christensen2001](#ref-Christensen2001)]. Christensen, Sinclair, Smith, and White established perturbation results for the similarity property and operator-algebraic invariants (Christensen, Sinclair, Smith, and White 2010) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[CSSW2010](#ref-CSSW2010)]; Cameron and collaborators then proved that the similarity property is equivalent to continuity of commutants, with the required uniformity over representations (Cameron et al. 2013) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[CCSSWW2013](#ref-CCSSWW2013)]. These results explain why ordinary norm closeness, control at all matrix levels, and implementation on the original Hilbert space must be kept distinct throughout a proof of strong stability. The companion manuscript (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[OpenAI2026Similarity](#ref-OpenAI2026Similarity)] also proves a uniform commutator estimate, using invariant tensors and a free-corner Hankel test. The amplification argument here instead uses free-word randomization and is proved independently. Neither commutator estimate alone constructs a nearby multiplicative correspondence between two algebras; Appendix <a href="#app:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:comparison">10</a> compares their scope and this remaining perturbation step.

The unit-ball metric supplies nearby elements but does not specify a linear or multiplicative correspondence. We must construct such a correspondence and control its implementation on the given Hilbert space, with estimates independent of the algebras and representations.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof overview

We first work on separable Hilbert spaces. For finite factors, two arguments meet in [sec:finite](#sec:finite). The first supplies matrix norm control: for a separably acting type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $M\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ and $h=h^*$, [thm:amplification](#thm:amplification) bounds all matrix amplifications of $x\mapsto[h,x]$ by an absolute multiple of its ordinary operator norm. It combines Haagerup’s cyclic similarity estimate (Haagerup 1983) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Haagerup1983](#ref-Haagerup1983)] and Popa’s independence theorem for tracial ultraproducts (Popa 2014) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Popa2014](#ref-Popa2014)] with a free-word randomization argument. The constant is independent of matrix size and representation.

A separate argument constructs an isomorphism close in ordinary norm to the inclusion. Using the common-position construction of (Cameron et al. 2014, Lemma 4.10) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[CCSSWW2014](#ref-CCSSWW2014)], we move the factors to a tracial representation in which one vector represents both traces, and the left and right actions of a common hyperfinite subfactor $R$ agree. The inclusion is irreducible, meaning $R'\cap M=\mathbb CI$.

For a countable strongly dense subgroup $G$ of the unitary group of $M$, choose nearby unitaries $v_g\in N$. Their multiplication errors are uniformly small. Section <a href="#sec:boundary" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:boundary">4</a> constructs a probability space $D$ with an amenable $G$-action preserving null sets. An adaptation of the averaging and polar-correction method for approximate representations (Kazhdan 1982; Burger, Ozawa, and Thom 2013) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Kazhdan1982](#ref-Kazhdan1982), [BurgerOzawaThom2013](#ref-BurgerOzawaThom2013)] corrects the nearby unitaries to measurable fields $W_g:D\to\mathcal U(N)$ satisfying $$W_g(x)W_h(g^{-1}x)=W_{gh}(x).$$ Here $G$ need not be amenable: the correction produces a cocycle over its amenable action, not a representation on the original space. On $D^2$, a tracial averaging argument and double ergodicity force a suitable equivariant vector field to be constant. We give the unitary-coefficient double-ergodicity argument in full, using the bilateral random-walk viewpoint of Kaimanovich; Björklund gives the corresponding unitary formulation and another proof (Kaimanovich 2003; Björklund 2014) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Kaimanovich2003](#ref-Kaimanovich2003), [Bjorklund2014](#ref-Bjorklund2014)]. The resulting linear bijection preserves adjoints and squares and is close to the inclusion; proximity excludes reversal of multiplication. Transporting this isomorphism back and applying the commutator estimate gives a small unitary on the original Hilbert space.

For infinite factors, a common copy of $\mathcal B(\ell^2)$ identifies the ordinary gap with the supremum of all matrix gaps. Tensoring with one fixed hyperfinite factor gives type $\mathrm{III}_1$ factors with an irreducible hyperfinite finite subfactor admitting a conditional expectation. This preparation uses Marrakchi’s absorption criterion for a trivial bicentralizer and Haagerup’s construction of the expected hyperfinite subfactor (Marrakchi 2020; Haagerup 1987) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Marrakchi2020](#ref-Marrakchi2020), [Haagerup1987](#ref-Haagerup1987)]. Houdayer and Marrakchi’s September 2026 preprint proves triviality of the bicentralizer for every type $\mathrm{III}_1$ factor with a faithful normal state (Houdayer and Marrakchi 2026, Theorem G) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[HoudayerMarrakchi2026](#ref-HoudayerMarrakchi2026)]; the preparation here uses only the earlier absorption criterion.

Small adjustments align the subfactor’s left and right actions and provide a common cyclic separating vector $\Omega$ whose two vector states centralize $R$. For either algebra, the closure of $x\Omega\mapsto x^*\Omega$ is its Tomita operator. [prop:modular-graphs](#prop:modular-graphs) shows that small matrix gaps force its two graphs to be close when the output is multiplied by any $c>0$, uniformly in $c$. This uniformity permits comparison of the modular actions. A uniform infinitesimal estimate for type $\mathrm{III}$ factors controls norm-continuous paths of unitaries whose conjugations nearly preserve the algebra. Using Arveson’s nest distance formula (Arveson 1975) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Arveson1975](#ref-Arveson1975)], analytic smoothing brings the modular comparison into this setting. Limits of conjugation maps yield, at each time, a unitary whose conjugation preserves one algebra and which is close to the other algebra’s modular unitary. Averaging corrects their multiplication law. Section <a href="#sec:modular-alignment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:modular-alignment">7</a> thereby obtains a single small change of position making the first algebra invariant under that modular group.

We form crossed products using this common action. Their unit balls are close at every matrix size, and continuous decomposition identifies one crossed product as a type $\mathrm{II}_\infty$ factor (Takesaki 1973, 2003) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Takesaki1973](#ref-Takesaki1973), [Takesaki2003II](#ref-Takesaki2003II)]. After aligning matrix units, its finite corner and the corresponding nearby corner are both finite factors. The finite-factor theorem conjugates these corners and hence the crossed products. Averaging gives a unital completely positive (UCP) map between the base algebras, meaning that it preserves the identity and positivity at every matrix size. A state slice gives a second UCP map between the original factors. The criterion in [prop:ucp](#prop:ucp), derived from Ricard–Roydor’s multiplication perturbation theorem (Ricard and Roydor 2014, Proposition 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[RicardRoydor2014](#ref-RicardRoydor2014)], turns both maps into small spatial isomorphisms. It does not require the averaging maps to be normal.

Finally, aligning centers and selecting measurable unitaries assembles the factor results on separable spaces. For an arbitrary Hilbert space, finite sets of operators and vectors lie in suitable separable restrictions. Extend their small conjugating unitaries by the identity. A subnet of the conjugation maps converges pointwise in the weak operator topology to a UCP map. The finite tests put its range in the required algebra, and the same criterion finishes the proof. All tolerances depend only on the requested final unitary displacement.

displays how the finite-factor result enters both the finite and infinite cases and how they meet in the final reductions.

<figure id="fig:proof">

<figcaption>The finite-factor theorem has two inputs and is reused in the crossed-product argument. The UCP criterion supplies pointwise normalizers in Section <a href="#sec:modular-alignment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:modular-alignment">7</a>, both descents in Section <a href="#sec:cores" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cores">8</a>, and the final limit in Section <a href="#sec:reductions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reductions">9</a>.</figcaption>
</figure>

Section <a href="#sec:foundations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:foundations">2</a> proves the transfer tools. Sections <a href="#sec:amplification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:amplification">3</a>–<a href="#sec:finite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite">5</a> establish the finite case. Sections <a href="#sec:modular-preparation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:modular-preparation">6</a>–<a href="#sec:cores" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cores">8</a> treat infinite factors. Section <a href="#sec:reductions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reductions">9</a> removes the factor and separability assumptions and gathers the final quantifiers. Appendix <a href="#app:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:comparison">10</a> distinguishes uniformity over representations from uniformity over factors and compares the commutator methods of this paper and the similarity companion.

<a id="sec:foundations"></a>
### Perturbation tools and a completely positive criterion

Several steps of the proof produce an averaged map instead of a homomorphism. The main result of this section, [prop:ucp](#prop:ucp), turns such a map into a small unitary conjugacy. All constants in this section are independent of the algebras and Hilbert spaces.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Metrics, amplification, and limits

We use the unit-ball formula <a href="#eq:kk-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:kk-definition">[eq:kk-definition]</a> also for unital $C^*$-algebras. For $A\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$, write $\mathcal U(A)$ for its unitary group, $A'=\{T\in\mathcal B(H):Ta=aT\text{ for every }a\in A\}$ for its commutant, and $Z(A)=A\cap A'$ for its center. The symbol $A\mathbin{\overline\otimes}B$ denotes the spatial von Neumann tensor product.

For a linear map $T:A\to\mathcal B(H)$, let $T_n$ act entrywise on $M_n(A)$, and put $\lVert T\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}=\sup_n\lVert T_n\rVert$. The same notation for rectangular matrices is obtained by adding zero rows and columns and compressing. For concretely represented von Neumann algebras define $$d_{\mathrm{cb}}(A,B)=\sup_{n\ge1}d(M_n(A),M_n(B)).$$ Here every matrix unit ball uses its operator norm on $H^n$. We write $A\subset_\gamma B$ if there is $\gamma'<\gamma$ such that every $x\in A_1$ has $\operatorname{dist}(x,B)\le\gamma'$; the approximant in $B$ need not be contractive. If $\lVert x\rVert\le1$ and $\lVert x-y\rVert\le r$, then $$y_0=\frac{y}{\max\{1,\lVert y\rVert\}}\in B_1,
 \qquad \lVert x-y_0\rVert\le2r.$$ We use this normalization whenever passing from near inclusions to unit-ball distances. Conversely, a strict upper bound for $d(A,B)$ gives both near inclusions with that bound. For a unitary $v$, <a id="eq:gap-adjustment"></a>$$\label{eq:gap-adjustment}
 \begin{aligned}
 d(A,vBv^*)&\le d(A,B)+2\lVert v-I\rVert,\\
 d_{\mathrm{cb}}(A,vBv^*)&\le d_{\mathrm{cb}}(A,B)+2\lVert v-I\rVert.
 \end{aligned}$$ These inequalities follow at every matrix level from $\lVert v_n x v_n^*-x\rVert\le2\lVert v-I\rVert\lVert x\rVert$. Also $\lVert v_1\cdots v_k-I\rVert\le\sum_j\lVert v_j-I\rVert$.

Inner products are linear in their first variable. A normal map between von Neumann algebras is an ultraweakly continuous map. A map is *unital completely positive*, abbreviated UCP, if it preserves the identity and each of its matrix amplifications is positive. Such maps are completely contractive.

We repeatedly use the following elementary compactness facts. A bounded operator ball is compact in the weak operator topology (WOT). A net of UCP maps with fixed domain and codomain $\mathcal B(H)$ has a subnet converging pointwise in WOT: use the product of the operator balls indexed by domain elements. The limit is UCP, since linearity, the identity, and every finite matrix positivity inequality pass to the limit. If the maps are within $a$ in cb norm of a fixed map, so is their limit; apply WOT closedness of norm balls at each fixed matrix size. If their values lie in a fixed von Neumann algebra, the limit has the same property. Normality need not pass to this limit and will not be required.

For clarity, $O(t)$ denotes a bound $Ct$ for an absolute constant $C$, valid below an absolute positive threshold for $t$. Constants may change between displays. A function tending to zero will always be chosen uniformly over the explicitly stated class of algebras.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### The amenable embedding theorem

The following form of Christensen’s theorem supplies the small changes of position used in the proof (Christensen 1980) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Christensen1980](#ref-Christensen1980)].

<a id="stmt-thm-amenable"></a>
<div id="thm:amenable" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Amenable near inclusions). *Let $R,Q\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be von Neumann algebras with identity $I$, with $R$ amenable. If $R\subset_\gamma Q$ and $\gamma<1/100$, there is a unitary $v\in(R\cup Q)''$ such that $$vRv^*\subseteq Q,\qquad \lVert v-I\rVert\le150\gamma.$$ If also $Q\subset_\gamma R$ and $\gamma<1/101$, the same conclusion holds with $vRv^*=Q$.*

</div>

This is (Cameron et al. 2014, Theorem 3.3(i)) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[CCSSWW2014](#ref-CCSSWW2014)], with its strict near-inclusion convention. The location of $v$ in $(R\cup Q)''$ is useful: any algebra commuting with both $R$ and $Q$ also commutes with the change of position. We apply the theorem to abelian algebras, hyperfinite finite factors, and type $\mathrm I$ algebras, all of which are amenable.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### From commutators to intertwiners

For $h\in\mathcal B(H)$ write $\operatorname{ad}_h(x)=[h,x]=hx-xh$.

<a id="stmt-lem-commutant-cb"></a>
<div id="lem:commutant-cb" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:commutant-cb"></a>
**Lemma 3**. *For every unital von Neumann algebra $A\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ and $h\in\mathcal B(H)$, $$\label{eq:commutant-cb}
 \operatorname{dist}(h,A')\le\lVert\operatorname{ad}_h|_A\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}.$$ Consequently $d_{\mathrm{cb}}(A,B)\le\gamma$ implies $d(A',B')\le4\gamma$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-commutant-cb"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-commutant-cb</summary>

*Proof.* Since $A'$ is weak-star closed, its preannihilator in the trace class computes the quotient norm $\operatorname{dist}(h,A')$. Let $\varphi$ be a trace-class functional of norm at most one vanishing on $A'$. A singular-value decomposition gives vectors $\xi,\eta\in\ell^2\otimes H$, each of norm at most one, with $$\varphi(x)=\langle (I\otimes x)\xi,\eta\rangle\quad(x\in\mathcal B(H)).$$ This uses only countably many vectors even when $H$ is nonseparable. Let $p$ project onto $\overline{(I\otimes A')\xi}$. This subspace reduces $I\otimes A'$, so every matrix entry of $p$ belongs to $A$. Moreover $p\xi=\xi$ and $p\eta=0$. Therefore $$\varphi(h)=\langle [I\otimes h,p]\xi,\eta\rangle.$$ If $q_n$ projects onto the first $n$ coordinates of $\ell^2$, the compression of this commutator is $[I_n\otimes h,q_npq_n]$, of norm at most $\lVert\operatorname{ad}_h|_A\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}$ because $q_npq_n\in M_n(A)_1$. Testing on finitely supported vectors and then using density proves the same bound for the full commutator. Taking the supremum over $\varphi$ proves <a href="#eq:commutant-cb" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:commutant-cb">[eq:commutant-cb]</a>.

For $h\in A'_1$, approximate each contraction in $M_n(B)$ by one in $M_n(A)$, with arbitrary slack above $\gamma$. It follows that $\lVert\operatorname{ad}_h|_B\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le2\gamma$. The first assertion gives distance at most $2\gamma$ to $B'$, and normalization of approximants gives distance at most $4\gamma$ to $B'_1$. Reverse the algebras to finish. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-paired"></a>
<div id="lem:paired" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Close representations). *Let $\rho,\theta:P\to\mathcal B(H)$ be normal unital $*$-representations of a von Neumann algebra. If $\lVert\rho-\theta\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le e<1/4$, there is a unitary $u\in\mathcal B(H)$ with $$u\rho(x)u^*=\theta(x)\quad(x\in P),\qquad \lVert u-I\rVert\le4e.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-paired"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-paired</summary>

*Proof.* If $e=0$, take $u=I$. Otherwise let $$\pi(x)=\begin{pmatrix}\rho(x)&0\\0&\theta(x)\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad S=\begin{pmatrix}0&I\\I&0\end{pmatrix}.$$ The von Neumann image $\pi(P)$ has the quotient matrix norms of $P$. The commutator of $S$ with $\pi$ has cb norm at most $e$, hence the same is true on $\pi(P)$ by quotient lifts with arbitrarily small norm slack. By [lem:commutant-cb](#lem:commutant-cb) choose $D\in\pi(P)'$ with $\lVert D-S\rVert<2e$. Its lower off-diagonal block $T$ satisfies $$T\rho(x)=\theta(x)T,\qquad \lVert T-I\rVert<2e<1.$$ Thus $T$ is invertible and $T^*T$ commutes with $\rho(P)$. Its polar unitary $u=T|T|^{-1}$ intertwines the representations. The singular values of $T$ lie between $1-r$ and $1+r$, where $r=\lVert T-I\rVert$, so $$\lVert u-I\rVert\le\lVert u-T\rVert+r=\lVert I-|T|\rVert+r\le2r<4e.$$ ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The completely positive criterion

<a id="eq:haagerup-products"></a>
The norm relevant to perturbing a product is the completely bounded norm on the Haagerup tensor product. We recall only the form needed here. For a bilinear map $b:P\times P\to P$, it is the least $K$ such that, at all rectangular sizes, $$\label{eq:haagerup-products}
 \left\|\left[\sum_{j=1}^r b(x_{ij},y_{jk})\right]_{i,k}\right\|
 \le K\lVert X\rVert\lVert Y\rVert,
 \quad X\in M_{p,r}(P),\quad Y\in M_{r,q}(P).$$ Indeed the matrix Haagerup norm is the infimum of $\lVert X\rVert\lVert Y\rVert$ over such row-column factorizations. We write $\lVert b\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}$ for this norm as well.

Write $m_P(x,y)=xy$ for the original multiplication on $P$. Correction of multiplication by cohomological methods goes back to Johnson and Raeburn–Taylor (Johnson 1977; Raeburn and Taylor 1977) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[Johnson1977](#ref-Johnson1977), [RaeburnTaylor1977](#ref-RaeburnTaylor1977)]. We use the completely bounded version supplied by Ricard–Roydor’s multiplication perturbation theorem (Ricard and Roydor 2014, Proposition 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[RicardRoydor2014](#ref-RicardRoydor2014)]: on a von Neumann algebra $P$, an associative product $m$ with $\lVert m-m_P\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le1/11$ admits a cb linear isomorphism $L$ with $$L(m(x,y))=L(x)L(y),\qquad
 \lVert L-\operatorname{id}\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le10\lVert m-m_P\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}.$$ If $m(x,y)^*=m(y^*,x^*)$, $L$ can preserve the involution. There is no normality or separability assumption on the new product.

<a id="stmt-prop-ucp"></a>
<div id="prop:ucp" class="proposition">

**Proposition 5** (UCP criterion). *Let $P,Q$ be von Neumann algebras, let $\rho:P\to\mathcal B(H)$ be a faithful normal unital representation, and let $Q\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ have the same identity. Suppose that $F:P\to Q$ is UCP and $$\lVert F-\rho\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le e<10^{-3},\qquad
 \sup_{y\in Q_1}\operatorname{dist}(y,\rho(P)_1)\le g,\qquad g+e<1.$$ Then there is a normal unital $*$-isomorphism $\theta:P\to Q$ and a unitary $u\in\mathcal B(H)$ such that $$\lVert\theta-\rho\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le32e,\qquad
 u\rho(x)u^*=\theta(x)\ (x\in P),\qquad \lVert u-I\rVert\le128e.$$ The map $F$ need not be normal.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-ucp"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-ucp</summary>

*Proof.* Complete isometry of $\rho$ gives $\lVert F_n(X)\rVert\ge(1-e)\lVert X\rVert$ at every matrix size. Thus $F$ is injective, its range $V$ is norm closed, and $\lVert F^{-1}:V\to P\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le(1-e)^{-1}$. For $y\in Q_1$, match $y$ with $\rho(x)$ for $x\in P_1$, allowing arbitrary slack, to obtain $\operatorname{dist}(y,V)\le g+e<1$. If $V$ were proper, the quotient map $Q\to Q/V$ would have norm one, contradicting this bound on $Q_1$. Hence $F$ is onto.

Transport multiplication from $Q$ to $P$: $$m(x,y)=F^{-1}(F(x)F(y)).$$ It is associative and preserves the involution. For rectangular $X,Y$, insert $\rho(XY)=\rho(X)\rho(Y)$ to see that $$\lVert F(XY)-F(X)F(Y)\rVert\le3e\lVert X\rVert\lVert Y\rVert.$$ Here the first difference is bounded by $e\lVert XY\rVert$ and the product difference by $2e\lVert X\rVert\lVert Y\rVert$, since $F$ and $\rho$ are complete contractions. Equation <a href="#eq:haagerup-products" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:haagerup-products">[eq:haagerup-products]</a> therefore gives $$q:=\lVert m-m_P\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le\frac{3e}{1-e}<\frac1{11}.$$ The cited theorem gives an involution-preserving $L$ with $\lVert L-\operatorname{id}\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le\lambda:=30e/(1-e)<1$. Then $\theta=FL^{-1}$ is a bijective $*$-homomorphism, and $$\lVert\theta-\rho\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}
 \le e+\lVert L^{-1}-\operatorname{id}\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}
 \le e+\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}
 = e+\frac{30e}{1-31e}\le32e.$$ Bijective multiplicativity makes $\theta$ unital. A $*$-isomorphism between von Neumann algebras is an order isomorphism on their selfadjoint parts, so it preserves bounded increasing suprema and is normal. Apply [lem:paired](#lem:paired), since $32e<1/4$. When $e=0$, the preceding range argument gives $Q=\rho(P)$ directly. ◻

</details>

We will use this criterion both to descend from a tensor product and to replace a pointwise weak limit by a spatial isomorphism. Its quantitative conclusion depends only on the cb error of the map; the ordinary reverse gap supplies surjectivity.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Tensor products and averaging

<a id="stmt-lem-tensor-gap"></a>
<div id="lem:tensor-gap" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6**. *Let $A,B\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be unital von Neumann algebras with $d_{\mathrm{cb}}(A,B)\le\gamma$. If $K$ is separable, then $$d_{\mathrm{cb}}(A\mathbin{\overline\otimes}\mathcal B(K),B\mathbin{\overline\otimes}\mathcal B(K))\le\gamma.$$ If $D\subseteq\mathcal B(K)$ is a von Neumann algebra whose commutant is the strong closure of increasing finite-dimensional unital algebras, then $$d_{\mathrm{cb}}(A\mathbin{\overline\otimes}D,B\mathbin{\overline\otimes}D)\le\gamma.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-tensor-gap"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-tensor-gap</summary>

*Proof.* Compress a contraction of $M_n(A\mathbin{\overline\otimes}\mathcal B(K))$ by increasing finite-rank projections on $K$. In a corner of rank $r$, choose an opposite contraction in $M_{nr}(B)$ within $\gamma$ plus arbitrarily small slack, and extend it by zero on the complement. The compressions of the original contraction converge strongly to it. A bounded WOT cluster of the opposite contractions is in $M_n(B\mathbin{\overline\otimes}\mathcal B(K))_1$ and is within $\gamma$, by WOT closedness of the norm bound, choosing the approximation slack to tend to zero along the compressions. Reverse the argument and take the supremum over $n$.

Now start with a contraction in $M_n(A\mathbin{\overline\otimes}D)$. The first assertion supplies a nearby contraction in $M_n(B\mathbin{\overline\otimes}\mathcal B(K))$. Average it by conjugation over the unitary group of each finite-dimensional stage of $D'$. The input commutes with these groups, so the bound is preserved. A WOT cluster of the averages commutes with $D'$ and still lies in $M_n(B\mathbin{\overline\otimes}\mathcal B(K))$. The von Neumann tensor commutation theorem (Blackadar 2006, Theorem III.4.5.8 and Corollary III.4.5.9, pp. 319–320) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[BlackadarOperatorAlgebras](#ref-BlackadarOperatorAlgebras)] places it in $M_n(B\mathbin{\overline\otimes}D)$. Reverse and amplify as before. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-invariant-mean"></a>
<div id="lem:invariant-mean" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Invariant operator means). *Let $G$ be a discrete amenable group and $m$ a left invariant mean on $\ell^\infty(G)$: thus $m$ is a positive unital linear functional with $m(f(h^{-1}\,\cdot))=m(f)$ for every $h\in G$. For a bounded family $T_g\in\mathcal B(K)$, define $$\langle (m_gT_g)\xi,\eta\rangle=m_g\langle T_g\xi,\eta\rangle.$$ This averaging map from bounded operator families to $\mathcal B(K)$ is UCP, commutes with multiplication on either side by a fixed operator, and preserves weak-operator closed linear subspaces. If $\lVert T_g-T\rVert\le a$ for every $g$, then $\lVert m_gT_g-T\rVert\le a$, also at rectangular matrix levels. No measurability of the family is required.*

*For a unitary representation $g\mapsto V_g$ on $K$, the map $$E_V(T)=m_gV_gTV_g^*\qquad(T\in\mathcal B(K))$$ is a UCP projection onto $\{V_g:g\in G\}'$. It preserves every von Neumann algebra invariant under these conjugations. More generally, for an action by automorphisms $\alpha_g$ on a von Neumann algebra $P$, the formula $$\varphi(E(x))=m_g\varphi(\alpha_g(x))\qquad(\varphi\in P_*)$$ defines a UCP map onto $P^G$ fixing that algebra. These averaging maps need not be normal.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-invariant-mean"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-invariant-mean</summary>

*Proof.* The coefficient formula defines a bounded sesquilinear form and hence an operator. Positivity at every matrix size and unitality follow by applying the positive scalar mean to matrix quadratic forms; the domain has the pointwise operations on bounded operator families. Fixed left and right factors pass through the coefficient formula. Testing the linear equations defining a weak-operator closed subspace proves range preservation. Testing vector coefficients at each rectangular size gives the norm bound. For the action average, left invariance makes the range invariant, while averaging fixes each invariant operator. An invariant algebra is preserved by the preceding range property. Predual duality gives the same argument for the general action on $P$, including preservation of matrix norm bounds. ◻

</details>

For $G=\mathbb R$ with the discrete topology, an invariant mean is obtained as a weak-star cluster point of uniform averages on finite boxes in finitely generated subgroups. Direct the choices by a finite set of translations and an error tolerance. Each subgroup is free abelian, and the relative boundary of its boxes tends to zero for every prescribed translation; the cluster is therefore positive, unital, and translation invariant.

The boundary means in [sec:boundary](#sec:boundary) require a separate construction: their equivariance involves a nonsingular action and their module coefficients vary over the boundary space.

<a id="sec:amplification"></a>
### Uniform amplification of commutators

The finite-factor argument will first produce an isomorphism that is close to the given representation in operator norm. To implement it by a small unitary, we need the same control at every matrix level. The following estimate supplies that passage.

<a id="stmt-thm-amplification"></a>
<div id="thm:amplification" class="theorem">

**Theorem 8** (Uniform amplification). *There is an absolute constant $C_{\mathrm{amp}}$ such that, for every type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $M\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ on a separable Hilbert space and every selfadjoint $h\in\mathcal B(H)$, $$\lVert\operatorname{ad}_h|_M\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le
 C_{\mathrm{amp}}\lVert\operatorname{ad}_h|_M\rVert.$$ The constant is independent of $M$, its representation, and $H$.*

</div>

We first control columns by the cyclic similarity theorem. A free-word estimate then permits us to apply this column bound to randomized matrix coefficients. The randomization also recovers a fixed positive multiple of the identity in a weak limit; this will detect every matrix commutator. The auxiliary matrix sizes depend on the matrix level being tested, but every estimate that survives to the conclusion is absolute.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Columns and trace limits

For $a_1,\ldots,a_l\in\mathcal B(H)$, write $\lVert(a_i)_i\rVert_{\mathrm{col}}=\lVert\sum_i a_i^*a_i\rVert^{1/2}$.

<a id="stmt-lem-column-commutator"></a>
<div id="lem:column-commutator" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9** (Column estimate). *Let $A\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra, let $h\in\mathcal B(H)$, and put $\delta=\lVert\operatorname{ad}_h|_A\rVert$. Then $$\lVert([h,a_i])_i\rVert_{\mathrm{col}}
 \le 8\delta\,\lVert(a_i)_i\rVert_{\mathrm{col}}
 \qquad(a_1,\ldots,a_l\in A).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-column-commutator"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-column-commutator</summary>

*Proof.* There is nothing to prove if $\delta=0$. Otherwise we use the triangular homomorphism associated with a derivation, as in (Haagerup 1983, 216) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[Haagerup1983](#ref-Haagerup1983)]: $$\Phi(a)=
 \begin{pmatrix}a&\delta^{-1}[h,a]\\0&a\end{pmatrix}
 \quad(a\in A).$$ This is a unital homomorphism with $\lVert\Phi\rVert\le2$. For $\xi\in H$, the closed subspace $$K_\xi=\overline{\{\Phi(a)(0,\xi):a\in A\}}
 \subseteq H\oplus H$$ is invariant for $\Phi(A)$, and the restricted homomorphism is cyclic. Haagerup’s cyclic similarity theorem gives $\lVert\Phi|_{K_\xi}\rVert_{\mathrm{cb}}\le\lVert\Phi|_{K_\xi}\rVert^3\le8$; see (Haagerup 1983, Theorem 1.1 and its proof, pp. 217, 225) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[Haagerup1983](#ref-Haagerup1983)]. Apply its column amplification to $(0,\xi)\in K_\xi$, and retain the upper component of each output. This gives $$\left(\sum_i\lVert[h,a_i]\xi\rVert^2\right)^{1/2}
 \le8\delta\,\lVert(a_i)_i\rVert_{\mathrm{col}}\lVert\xi\rVert.$$ Taking the supremum over unit vectors proves the claim. ◻

</details>

We use normalized traces on finite factors and the standard comparison and subdivision of projections by trace (Blackadar 2006, 122:III.1.7.9–10, p. 257; III.2.5.4(iii) and Theorem III.2.5.7, p. 279) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[BlackadarOperatorAlgebras](#ref-BlackadarOperatorAlgebras)]. For a finite von Neumann algebra $(P,\tau)$, put $\lVert x\rVert_2=\tau(x^*x)^{1/2}$ and $\lVert x\rVert_1=\tau(|x|)$. Fix a free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$. The tracial ultrapower $P^\omega$ is the quotient of the bounded sequences in $P$ by those with $\lim_{j\to\omega}\lVert x_j\rVert_2=0$; its trace is $\tau_\omega((x_j)_\omega)=\lim_{j\to\omega}\tau(x_j)$. We identify $P$ with its constant sequences.

<a id="stmt-lem-trace-transfer"></a>
<div id="lem:trace-transfer" class="lemma">

**Lemma 10** (Transfer from the trace to a normal representation). *Let $P\subseteq\mathcal B(K)$ be a finite von Neumann algebra with faithful normal trace $\tau$.*

1.  *If $(x_j)$ is bounded and $\lim_{j\to\omega}\lVert x_j\rVert_2=0$, then $\lim_{j\to\omega}\lVert x_j\xi\rVert=0$ for every $\xi\in K$.*

2.  *If $x=(x_j)_\omega\in P^\omega$, then the weak-operator ultralimit of $x_j$ in this representation is $E_P(x)$, where $E_P:P^\omega\to P$ is the trace-preserving conditional expectation.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-trace-transfer"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-trace-transfer</summary>

*Proof.* The functionals $a\mapsto\tau(ba)$, $b\in P$, are norm dense in the predual $P_*$. Indeed, their annihilator in $(P_*)^*=P$ is zero, by faithfulness of the trace, so Hahn–Banach gives the density. For a normal vector functional $\varphi$, approximate $\varphi$ in norm by one of these functionals. If $\lVert x_j\rVert\le K_0$, then $$|\varphi(x_j^*x_j)|
 \le K_0^2\lVert\varphi-\tau(b\,\cdot)\rVert
       +\lVert b\rVert\lVert x_j\rVert_2^2.$$ This proves (i). For (ii), the defining identity of the conditional expectation gives $$\lim_{j\to\omega}\tau(bx_j)
 =\tau_\omega(bx)=\tau(bE_P(x))\qquad(b\in P).$$ The same predual-density argument, using boundedness, extends the identity to every normal functional. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### Free projection words

The following estimate controls sums of projection words whose labels have the same multiplicities. In the later randomization, these sums will give coefficients to which we apply the column estimate separately. When the squared estimates are added, each diagonal vector component is counted once for every multiset containing its label. We therefore need squared word-norm bounds with a uniformly bounded sum through each fixed label.

<a id="stmt-lem-free-words"></a>
<div id="lem:free-words" class="lemma">

**Lemma 11** (Free projection words). *For every integer $r\geq 1$ there are constants $C_r>0$ and an integer $d_r\geq r$ with the following property. Let $d\geq d_r$, and let $(\mathcal A,\tau)$ be a von Neumann algebra with a faithful normal tracial state. Suppose that $A_1,\ldots,A_r\subset\mathcal A$ are unital copies of $M_d(\mathbb C)$ which are scalar free: the trace of a product of centered letters from successively different copies is zero. For each $j$, let $(e_a^{(j)})_{a=1}^d$ be the diagonal minimal projections of $A_j$.*

<a id="eq:fw-beta"></a>
*For a multiset $W$ of cardinality $r$ drawn from $\{1,\ldots,d\}$, put $$B_W=\sum_{\substack{(a_1,\ldots,a_r)\in\{1,\ldots,d\}^r\\
                     \text{with multiset }W}}
       e_{a_r}^{(r)}\cdots e_{a_1}^{(1)}.$$ Then $\lVert B_W\rVert\leq\beta_W$, where $$\label{eq:fw-beta}
 \beta_W=
 \begin{cases}
  7\sqrt{(r-1)!}\,d^{-(r-1)/2},
       &\text{if all labels in $W$ are distinct},\\
  C_r d^{-(r-1)/2},&\text{otherwise}.
 \end{cases}$$ The constants and threshold depend only on $r$, not on the ambient algebra, the free copies, or $W$.*

</div>

Every word in $B_W$ ends with a projection $e_a^{(1)}$ whose label occurs in $W$. Thus $B_W$ has right support in the sum of those projections from the first copy, at most $r$ of them. This support bound will let us reduce each coefficient to a column, once the auxiliary matrix dimension is large enough.

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-free-words"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-free-words</summary>

*Proof.* We estimate the centered words first, then restore their scalar parts. The decomposition below uses the creation, annihilation, and diagonal actions in the free-product Hilbert space, as in the framework developed by Voiculescu (Voiculescu 1985) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[Voiculescu1985](#ref-Voiculescu1985)] and the reduced-free-product inequalities of Ricard and Xu (Ricard and Xu 2006, sec. 1 and Fact 2.6) <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[RicardXu2006](#ref-RicardXu2006)]. The corresponding bound for words of fixed length in free groups is Haagerup’s inequality (Haagerup 1979, Lemma 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[Haagerup1979](#ref-Haagerup1979)]. Here the prescribed label multiplicities require the additional tuple-matrix and square-summation estimates proved below.

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
###### Reduced words and their cuts.

We first describe the Hilbert space on which the word norm will be estimated. Replace $\mathcal A$ by $W^*(A_1,\ldots,A_r)$ and use its faithful left representation on $L^2(\mathcal A,\tau)$. Set $$H_j^\circ=L^2(A_j,\tau)\ominus\mathbb C1.$$ Freeness identifies this representation space with <a id="eq:fw-fock"></a>$$\label{eq:fw-fock}
 \mathcal F=\mathbb C\Omega\ \oplus\!
 \bigoplus_{\substack{\ell\geq1,\ i_1,\ldots,i_\ell\in\{1,\ldots,r\}\\
                      i_t\ne i_{t+1}}}
 H_{i_1}^\circ\otimes\cdots\otimes H_{i_\ell}^\circ.$$ Indeed, map a tensor to the corresponding product applied to $1$. Its inner products are the tensor inner products: in the trace of the adjoint of one reduced word times another, pair the adjacent letters at the junction, split their product into its trace and centered part, and iterate. Freeness eliminates the centered terms until the copy sequences agree and all letters have been paired. Distinct summands are orthogonal. Splitting each letter into its scalar and centered parts shows that reduced words span the algebra generated by the copies, hence a dense subspace of $L^2(\mathcal A,\tau)$.

Write $h_a^{(j)}=e_a^{(j)}-d^{-1}1$. Define the label map $$D_j:\ell^2(\{1,\ldots,d\})\longrightarrow H_j^\circ,
 \qquad D_j\delta_a=h_a^{(j)}.$$ The normalized matrix trace gives <a id="eq:fw-label-map"></a>$$\label{eq:fw-label-map}
 D_j^*D_j=\frac1d I-\frac1{d^2}\mathbf1\mathbf1^*,
 \qquad \lVert D_j\rVert\leq d^{-1/2},$$ where $\mathbf1$ is the vector all of whose entries are one.

<a id="eq:fw-preservation-operator"></a>
Left multiplication by $h_a^{(j)}$ creates the first letter $h_a^{(j)}$ on a reduced tensor not beginning in copy $j$. On a tensor $b\otimes\xi$ beginning in that copy, it has two parts: annihilation, equal to $\tau(h_a^{(j)}b)\xi$, and preservation of the first leg, with operator $$\label{eq:fw-preservation-operator}
 K_a^{(j)}
 =P_j^\circ L_{e_a^{(j)}}P_j^\circ
       -\frac1d I_{H_j^\circ}
 \quad\text{on }H_j^\circ.$$ Here $P_j^\circ$ is the orthogonal projection onto $H_j^\circ$ inside $L^2(A_j,\tau)$, and $L$ denotes left multiplication. These formulas also apply to the vacuum, on which only creation occurs.

Consider a fully centered word $h_{a_r}^{(r)}\cdots h_{a_1}^{(1)}$. The rightmost letter acts first. Since the copy indices $1,\ldots,r$ are distinct, once a creation or preservation occurs, every later operation must be a creation. Thus the word is a sum of cuts of two kinds: $p$ annihilations followed by $q=r-p$ creations, or $p$ annihilations, one preservation, and $q=r-p-1$ creations.

For completeness, the domain restrictions in these cuts can be made explicit. For a set $S$ of copy indices, let $\mathcal F_{\notin S}$ denote the vacuum plus all reduced tensors whose first copy index is outside $S$. For a cut without preservation put $$S_p=\bigl(\{p\}\text{ if }p\geq1\bigr)
       \cup\bigl(\{p+1\}\text{ if }p<r\bigr),
 \qquad E_p=\mathcal F_{\notin S_p}.$$ Its source and target spaces are canonically embedded copies of $$H_1^\circ\otimes\cdots\otimes H_p^\circ\otimes E_p,
 \qquad
 H_r^\circ\otimes\cdots\otimes H_{p+1}^\circ\otimes E_p,$$ respectively; empty tensor products mean $\mathbb C$. Excluding copy $p$ makes the input reduced, and excluding copy $p+1$ permits the first creation. For a preservation cut put $j=p+1$ and $E=\mathcal F_{\notin\{j\}}$. Its source and target spaces are $$H_1^\circ\otimes\cdots\otimes H_p^\circ\otimes H_j^\circ\otimes E,
 \qquad
 H_r^\circ\otimes\cdots\otimes H_{j+1}^\circ\otimes H_j^\circ\otimes E.$$ All embeddings are isometries; a cut is zero on the orthogonal complement of its indicated source space. In particular these restrictions depend on the cut alone and never on the labels.

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
###### Distinct labels and tuple matrices.

<a id="eq:fw-cut-factorization"></a>
We now suppose that $W$ has distinct labels and sum the fully centered words with multiset $W$. In each cut, the annihilation map is the tensor product of $D_1^*,\ldots,D_p^*$, with the identity on the remaining factors. The creation map uses the $D_j$ in decreasing copy order. Their norms are at most $d^{-p/2}$ and $d^{-q/2}$. Between them is a matrix on label tuples, scalar-valued without preservation and operator-valued on $H_j^\circ$ with preservation. Tensor order reversals are unitary. Together with the isometric source and target embeddings, this gives a factorization of each cut whose norm is bounded by $$\label{eq:fw-cut-factorization}
 d^{-(p+q)/2}
       \lVert\text{matrix on label tuples}\rVert.$$ The tail factor carries the identity throughout.

<a id="eq:fw-no-preservation"></a>
Without preservation, the tuple matrix has entry one exactly when the input and output tuples together list $W$. Group tuples by their underlying subsets. Each input subset of size $p$ is paired only with its complementary output subset of size $q$, giving mutually orthogonal all-ones blocks of size $q!$ by $p!$. The matrix norm is therefore $\sqrt{p!q!}$. Summing these cuts gives at most $$\label{eq:fw-no-preservation}
 \sum_{p=0}^r\sqrt{p!(r-p)!}\,d^{-r/2}
 \leq(r+1)\sqrt{r!}\,d^{-r/2}.$$

For a preservation cut, $p+q=r-1$. Summing and replicating over the orders of the input and output tuples introduces a factor $\sqrt{p!q!}$. The remaining matrix is indexed by subsets $B\subset W$ of size $p$ on the input and subsets $A\subset W$ of size $q$ on the output. Its entry is $K_a^{(j)}$ when $A\cap B$ is empty and $\{a\}=W\setminus(A\cup B)$; other entries vanish.

<a id="eq:fw-preservation-sum"></a>
To estimate this matrix, first replace $K_a^{(j)}$ by the projection $L_{e_a^{(j)}}$ on $L^2(A_j,\tau)$ and call the resulting matrix $T$. For a fixed row $A$, the admissible columns $B$ give distinct missing labels $a$, so their output ranges are orthogonal. For a fixed column $B$, the admissible rows also give distinct $a$, so the projection domains are orthogonal. Thus, for an input $(\xi_B)_B$, $$\lVert T\xi\rVert^2
 =\sum_A\sum_{\substack{B:\ A\cap B=\varnothing}}
      \lVert L_{e_{a(A,B)}^{(j)}}\xi_B\rVert^2
 \leq\sum_B\lVert\xi_B\rVert^2.$$ Consequently $\lVert T\rVert\leq1$. Compressing every input and output leg to $H_j^\circ$ preserves this bound. By <a href="#eq:fw-preservation-operator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fw-preservation-operator">[eq:fw-preservation-operator]</a>, the remaining scalar correction is $d^{-1}$ times the zero-one matrix of admissible pairs, tensored with $I_{H_j^\circ}$. This matrix has $p+1$ entries in each row and $q+1$ in each column; Cauchy–Schwarz in each row bounds its norm by $\sqrt{(p+1)(q+1)}$. The preservation tuple matrix therefore has norm at most $$\sqrt{p!q!}
       \left(1+\frac{\sqrt{(p+1)(q+1)}}d\right)
 \leq\sqrt{p!q!}\left(1+\frac rd\right).$$ Using <a href="#eq:fw-cut-factorization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fw-cut-factorization">[eq:fw-cut-factorization]</a> and summing the preservation cuts gives $$\label{eq:fw-preservation-sum}
 \left(1+\frac rd\right)\sqrt{(r-1)!}\,d^{-(r-1)/2}
       \sum_{p=0}^{r-1}\binom{r-1}{p}^{-1/2}.$$

The last sum is at most five, uniformly in $r$. To see this, put $m=r-1$. For $m=0,1,2,3$ the sums are respectively $1$, $2$, $2+1/\sqrt2$, and $2+2/\sqrt3$. For $m\geq4$, unimodality of the binomial coefficients gives $$\sum_{p=0}^m\binom mp^{-1/2}
 \leq2+\frac2{\sqrt m}
         +(m-3)\sqrt{\frac2{m(m-1)}}
 \leq3+\sqrt2<5.$$

<a id="pm-h-16"></a>
###### Scalar parts and repeated labels.

<a id="eq:fw-single-word"></a>
It remains to restore the scalar parts of the projections and to treat repeated labels. We record a bound for a single centered word of length $l\geq1$ in distinct copy positions, with arbitrary labels. The same cuts now have scalar coefficients rather than tuple sums. Each creation or annihilation has norm at most $d^{-1/2}$, and each preservation has norm at most one, since it is a compression of multiplication by $e_a-d^{-1}1$. There are $l$ preservation cuts and $l+1$ cuts without preservation. Hence $$\label{eq:fw-single-word}
 \lVert h_{a_l}^{(i_l)}\cdots h_{a_1}^{(i_1)}\rVert
 \leq l\,d^{-(l-1)/2}+(l+1)d^{-l/2}
 \leq(2l+1)d^{-(l-1)/2}.$$ This estimate does not require the labels to be distinct.

<a id="eq:fw-scalar-remainder"></a>
Expand a projection word of length $r$ using $e_a=d^{-1}1+h_a$. A term retaining $l<r$ centered letters contains the scalar factor $d^{-(r-l)}$. For $1\leq l<r$, <a href="#eq:fw-single-word" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fw-single-word">[eq:fw-single-word]</a> bounds it by $$(2l+1)d^{-r+(l+1)/2}\leq(2l+1)d^{-r/2}.$$ The term retaining no centered letters has norm $d^{-r}\leq d^{-r/2}$. Thus the difference between $B_W$ and its fully centered sum has norm at most $$\label{eq:fw-scalar-remainder}
 r! H_r d^{-r/2},
 \qquad
 H_r=1+\sum_{l=1}^{r-1}\binom rl(2l+1),$$ because at most $r!$ ordered tuples have multiset $W$.

For distinct labels, combining <a href="#eq:fw-no-preservation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fw-no-preservation">[eq:fw-no-preservation]</a>, <a href="#eq:fw-preservation-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fw-preservation-sum">[eq:fw-preservation-sum]</a>, and <a href="#eq:fw-scalar-remainder" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fw-scalar-remainder">[eq:fw-scalar-remainder]</a> yields $$\lVert B_W\rVert
 \leq5\left(1+\frac rd\right)
           \sqrt{(r-1)!}\,d^{-(r-1)/2}
       +J_r d^{-r/2},
 \qquad J_r=(r+1)\sqrt{r!}+r!H_r.$$ For example, choose an integer $$d_r\geq\max\left\{5r,\frac{J_r^2}{(r-1)!}\right\}.$$ Then the two excess terms, after division by $\sqrt{(r-1)!}\,d^{-(r-1)/2}$, are each at most one, proving the constant seven in <a href="#eq:fw-beta" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fw-beta">[eq:fw-beta]</a>.

For repeated labels, apply <a href="#eq:fw-single-word" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fw-single-word">[eq:fw-single-word]</a> to every fully centered summand and use <a href="#eq:fw-scalar-remainder" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fw-scalar-remainder">[eq:fw-scalar-remainder]</a>. Since $d^{-r/2}\leq d^{-(r-1)/2}$, it suffices to take $$C_r=r!\bigl(2r+1+H_r\bigr).$$ These choices depend only on $r$ and complete the proof. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-cor-through-label"></a>
<div id="cor:through-label" class="corollary">

<a id="eq:fw-through-label"></a>
**Corollary 12** (Sum of squared word bounds through one label). *For every integer $N\geq1$ there is an integer $d_0(N)$ such that, whenever $d\geq d_0(N)$, the constants <a href="#eq:fw-beta" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fw-beta">[eq:fw-beta]</a> satisfy $$\label{eq:fw-through-label}
 \max_{1\leq a\leq d}
 \sum_{\substack{W\text{ a multiset from }\{1,\ldots,d\}\\
                 |W|=r,\ a\in W}}
       \beta_W^2\leq64
 \qquad(1\leq r\leq N).$$ Membership $a\in W$ here means that $a$ occurs at least once; each multiset is counted once, regardless of that multiplicity. In particular, for free copies as in <a href="#lem:free-words" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:free-words">11</a>, the norm bounds and <a href="#eq:fw-through-label" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fw-through-label">[eq:fw-through-label]</a> hold simultaneously through length $N$ after choosing this one threshold for $d$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-through-label"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-through-label</summary>

*Proof.* Fix $r$ and a label $a$. There are $\binom{d-1}{r-1}$ distinct-label multisets containing $a$, and their contribution is $$49(r-1)!d^{-(r-1)}\binom{d-1}{r-1}\leq49.$$ For $r=1$ this is the entire sum. Suppose $r\geq2$.
